The [API reference](https://docs.opentyphoon.ai/en/api-reference/) and the [quick start](https://docs.opentyphoon.ai/en/quickstart/) set the base to `https://api.opentyphoon.ai/v1` and document `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`. Arca stores Chat Completions on `https://api.opentyphoon.ai/v1` (that base plus `/chat/completions`). No Responses, Anthropic Messages, or Gemini contract is documented, so none is stored.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` returns a bare JSON array, not an OpenAI `{object: "list", data: [...]}` envelope. That array includes `typhoon-v2.5-30b-a3b-instruct` and OCR and ASR ids. It does not include `typhoon-v2.1-12b-instruct`. The quick start example and the [models page](https://docs.opentyphoon.ai/en/models/) still show `typhoon-v2.1-12b-instruct`, and that id is not treated as verified online from the public list. No reviewed HTTP discovery spec is registered, and the generic OpenAI `/v1/models` decoder is not used. With or without a credential, Arca does not call the network. The seed is `typhoon-v2.5-30b-a3b-instruct`. The models page does not give that id a separate display name or an integer context length, so the seed stores neither. OCR and ASR ids are not seeded. An explicit Models list URL on a connection still uses the OpenAI catalog decoder.

The [FAQ](https://docs.opentyphoon.ai/en/faq/) says the Typhoon API is a research showcase and is free to use. The models page lists 5 requests per second and 200 requests per minute for `typhoon-v2.5-30b-a3b-instruct`. The catalog does not encode that quota. The quick start says to create a key at Playground › API Keys, <https://playground.opentyphoon.ai/api-key>. The template portal is that URL with kind `api-key`.

Only `api.opentyphoon.ai` is mapped for host inference. `opentyphoon.ai` is the product site, `docs.opentyphoon.ai` is the documentation site, and `playground.opentyphoon.ai` is the key page. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes.

## Regolo — 2026-11-10

`regolo` / `Regolo` is a gateway Provider for [Regolo](https://docs.regolo.ai/getting-started/quick-start/). The [quick start](https://docs.regolo.ai/getting-started/quick-start/) sets the OpenAI client base to `https://api.regolo.ai/v1` and calls `POST /v1/chat/completions`. Arca stores Chat Completions on `https://api.regolo.ai/v1` (that base plus `/chat/completions`). No Responses or Anthropic Messages contract is documented, so neither is stored. No Gemini contract is documented, so Gemini is not stored.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` returns a public list of 16 entries. The list includes the four Core chat ids below and also non-chat rows, including `deepseek-ocr-2`, `faster-whisper-large-v3`, `Qwen-Image`, `Qwen3-Embedding-8B`, and `Qwen3-Reranker-4B`. The generic OpenAI decoder would import those rows, so no reviewed HTTP discovery spec is registered and the generic `/v1/models` fallback is not used. With or without a credential, Arca does not call the network. The seed is the four ids from the [pricing](https://regolo.ai/pricing/) Core table that also appear in that list, in that order: `qwen3.8-27b`, `gemma4-31b`, `glm5.2`, and `gpt-oss-120b`. Those pages do not publish separate display names or integer context lengths for these ids, so the seed stores neither. The quick start example uses `Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct`. That id is not one of the four reviewed Core seeds and is not seeded. An explicit Models list URL on a connection still uses the OpenAI catalog decoder.

The pricing page says the trial is 30 days, with no credit card and no commitment, at 1M tokens per day across the Core models. After the trial the account picks a plan. The catalog does not encode that trial. The quick start says to sign up at <https://dashboard.regolo.ai> and create a key in Virtual Keys. No direct Virtual Keys URL is published, so the template portal is <https://dashboard.regolo.ai> with kind `console`.

Only `api.regolo.ai` is mapped for host inference. `regolo.ai` is the marketing site, `docs.regolo.ai` and `docs.api.regolo.ai` are documentation hosts, and `dashboard.regolo.ai` is the account app. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes.

## Itzi — 2026-11-10

`itzi` / `Itzi` is a gateway Provider for [Itzi](https://itzi.app/developers). The [developers page](https://itzi.app/developers) documents `POST /v1/responses`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, and `POST /v1/messages`. OpenAI clients use `https://itzi.app/v1`. The Anthropic SDK and Claude Code use `https://itzi.app`, and the client appends `/v1/messages`. The same page says Responses is the best fit for new OpenAI-compatible integrations. Arca uses Responses as the default on `https://itzi.app/v1`, stores Chat Completions on that same base, and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://itzi.app`. No Gemini contract is documented, so Gemini is not stored. Requests are stateless. `previous_response_id` is not implemented, and the caller sends prior message and function items on each turn. Arca has no Responses storage override to declare. Keys begin with `itzi_sk_`. The page accepts `Authorization: Bearer` or `x-api-key`.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` returns HTTP 401, so the list is not a public catalog. Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential. With no credential, Arca does not call the network. The seed is the current promo id `special-free/deepseek-v4.1-flash`. The [free DeepSeek page](https://itzi.app/free-deepseek) and the developers banner describe that id as a time-limited offer, not the full catalog and not a permanent free tier. The offer runs from 2026-11-02 00:00 UTC through 2026-11-17 00:00 UTC. The base allowance is 20 requests per day, up to 150 with a campaign code. The allowance resets at 00:00 UTC and unused requests do not carry over. A request allows at most 64,000 input tokens and 12,000 total output tokens, including reasoning and tool steps. Web Search, Python Studio, and prompt caching are outside the offer. No payment plan or top-up is required for the offer. The catalog has no free or quota field, so none of that is encoded. The seed stores no display name and no context length. A saved key loads `GET /v1/models`. This template is separate from the official DeepSeek API at `api.deepseek.com`.

Only `itzi.app` is mapped for host inference. The site and the API share that host. `www.itzi.app` and `api.itzi.app` stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. `api.deepseek.com` stays `deepseek`. The developers page links key management at <https://itzi.app/app/api>. The template portal is that URL with kind `api-key`.

## Gonka Broker — 2026-11-10

`gonka-broker` / `Gonka Broker` is a gateway Provider for [Gonka Broker](https://docs.gonkabroker.com/reference/api-compatibility/). It is a different product from `gonkarouter` (`https://api.gonkarouter.io/v1` on gonkarouter.io). The [OpenAI compatibility](https://docs.gonkabroker.com/reference/api-compatibility/) page sets the base to `https://proxy.gonkabroker.com/v1` and documents `POST /v1/chat/completions`. The [Anthropic compatibility](https://docs.gonkabroker.com/reference/anthropic-api/) page uses `https://proxy.gonkabroker.com`, and the client appends `/v1/messages`. Responses is not a supported route. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://proxy.gonkabroker.com/v1` and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://proxy.gonkabroker.com`. Legacy completions and embeddings are documented and are not Arca chat protocols, so they are not stored. Gemini is not stored. Keys start with `gnk-prx-`.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` returns four rows: `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M2.7`, `zai-org/GLM-5.3-Flash`, `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731`, and the embedding model `BAAI/bge-m3`. The docs say an embedding model returns HTTP 404 on chat. The generic OpenAI decoder would import `BAAI/bge-m3`, so no reviewed HTTP discovery spec is registered and the generic `/v1/models` fallback is not used. With or without a credential, Arca does not call the network. The seed is the three chat ids, in that order. Those pages do not publish separate display names or integer context lengths, so the seed stores neither. `BAAI/bge-m3` is not seeded. An explicit Models list URL on a connection still uses the OpenAI catalog decoder.

The [free LLM page](https://gonkabroker.com/free-llm-api/) says each account receives at least 1M tokens per month, priced against the most expensive model at the time, so a cheaper model yields more tokens. The grant renews on the first of the month and unused tokens do not accumulate. No credit card is required. The [billing guide](https://docs.gonkabroker.com/guides/billing-and-deposits/) says the free tier is bound to a verified mobile number, with a one-time SMS code, one tier per number, and no VoIP or landline numbers. The grant is issued after verification and renewed at the start of each calendar month. Free funds are spent before paid balance. The catalog does not encode that grant. [Create an API key](https://docs.gonkabroker.com/getting-started/create-api-key/) says to sign in at <https://app.gonkabroker.com> and open the API Keys tab. No direct tab URL is published, so the template portal is <https://app.gonkabroker.com> with kind `console`.

Only `proxy.gonkabroker.com` is mapped for host inference. `gonkabroker.com` is the marketing site, `docs.gonkabroker.com` is the documentation site, and `app.gonkabroker.com` is the account app. `api.gonkarouter.io` stays `gonkarouter`. Those other hosts stay `custom`, as do lookalikes.

## Fortytwo Engine — 2026-11-10

`fortytwo-engine` / `Fortytwo Engine` is a gateway Provider for [Fortytwo Engine](https://engine.fortytwo.network/), Fortytwo's OpenAI-compatible inference API for open-weight models. The [quickstart](https://docs.fortytwo.network/docs/quickstart), [API conventions](https://docs.fortytwo.network/apis/api-reference-intro), and [OpenAI compatibility](https://docs.fortytwo.network/docs/openai-compatibility) set the base URL to `https://api.fortytwo.network/v1`. They document `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`. The OpenAI SDK examples use that base URL. Arca stores Chat Completions on `https://api.fortytwo.network/v1` (that base plus `/chat/completions`). [Get access](https://docs.fortytwo.network/docs/access) says keys start with `sk-ft-v1-` and are sent on `Authorization`. It does not document `x-api-key`.

[OpenAI compatibility](https://docs.fortytwo.network/docs/openai-compatibility) lists `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `GET /v1/models` as supported. It says Responses, Completions, Embeddings, Audio, Images, Files, Batches, Fine-tuning, Moderations, Assistants, and `GET /v1/models/{id}` are not supported (`404`). The [Codex guide](https://docs.fortytwo.network/agents/codex) says Codex needs the Responses API, so it runs a local LiteLLM proxy that translates Responses to Chat Completions. No Anthropic Messages or Gemini generateContent contract is documented. Arca does not store Responses, Anthropic Messages, or Gemini.

Probes on 2026-11-10: `GET /v1/models` and `POST /v1/chat/completions` with no key return HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key` (`The API key is missing or not valid.`). A bogus Bearer key on those routes returns that same 401. A bogus `x-api-key` on both routes returns that same 401 text, so this probe does not show whether `x-api-key` was read. `POST /v1/responses` and `POST /v1/messages` with no key, and the same requests with a bogus Bearer key, return HTTP 404 with an empty body. `POST /v1/messages` with a bogus `x-api-key` returns that empty 404. `POST /v1/completions` and `POST /v1/embeddings` with a bogus Bearer key return that empty 404. `GET /v1/nonexistent` returns that empty 404 with no key and with a bogus Bearer key. `GET /v1/models/qwen/qwen3.8-27b` with no key, and `POST /v1beta/models/qwen/qwen3.8-27b:generateContent` with no key and with `x-goog-api-key`, return that empty 404. `OPTIONS /v1/chat/completions` returns HTTP 405 with an empty body. `GET /`, `GET /models`, and `POST /messages` return HTTP 404 `404 page not found` (`text/plain`). Responses, Completions, Embeddings, Messages, and Gemini are not stored.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential. [List models](https://docs.fortytwo.network/apis/get-models) and the [models guide](https://docs.fortytwo.network/docs/models) document `GET /v1/models` as `{object: "list", data: [{id, object: "model", created, owned_by}]}` and require a bearer key. The published example is `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` with `owned_by` `fortytwo`. The live authenticated body was not observed. The decoder reads `id` and `name`. Documented entries have no `name` and no context field, so a model discovered with a key does not carry a context length from this list. With no credential, Arca does not call the network. The seed is the only row on the models page: `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` (Qwen3.8-27B), context 262,144. That page also states max output 235,929. The catalog summary has no max-output field, so 235,929 is not stored. The [engine page](https://engine.fortytwo.network/) prints the same context window and max output.

The models page says this model accepts text and image input, text output, streaming, reasoning effort, tool calling including parallel calls, and structured output `json_object` or `json_schema` with `strict: true`. [Image input](https://docs.fortytwo.network/docs/images) accepts PNG and JPEG by HTTPS URL or base64 data URL. The engine page also lists WebP. The image guide does not, so WebP is not treated as supported. The catalog does not encode features.

Pricing disagrees across official pages, so no price is stored and this model is not a permanent free tier. [Pricing](https://docs.fortytwo.network/docs/pricing) lists `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` at $0.425 / 1M input tokens, $0.085 / 1M cached input tokens, and $2.55 / 1M output tokens, and says reasoning tokens are billed as output. The engine page model specs show cache read, input, and output at $0 / MTok and do not state a duration. The official [@fortytwo announcement](https://x.com/fortytwo/status/2120138790853595138) says Qwen3.8-27B is free on Fortytwo Engine for 24 hours from 2026-11-10 12:00 UTC, at any volume, with no card. [Promotional credits](https://docs.fortytwo.network/docs/promotional-credits) describes promo codes and offers and does not name this model or that 24-hour window. The catalog does not encode either published price or that time-limited announcement.

[Tiers and limits](https://docs.fortytwo.network/docs/limits) says every account starts on Core: 20 requests per minute and 500 per hour, per model. Pro is 200 / 7,000 after $500 of spend in a calendar month, and Scale is 600 / 20,000 after $10,000. The engine page prints those same three rate-limit rows. The catalog does not encode the quota.

Only `api.fortytwo.network` is mapped for host inference. `engine.fortytwo.network` serves the product site (`GET /v1/models` there is the site HTML, HTTP 404). `fortytwo.network` is the marketing site, and `www.fortytwo.network` redirects to it. `docs.fortytwo.network` is the documentation site. `platform.fortytwo.network` is the account app. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. `www.api.fortytwo.network` did not resolve. The platform bundle's static routes include `/api-keys`. An unsigned GET of <https://platform.fortytwo.network/api-keys> returns the login page with `redirect=/api-keys`. The template portal is that URL with kind `api-key`.

## vLLM Semantic Router — 2026-11-11

`vllm-semantic-router` / `vLLM Semantic Router` is a gateway Provider for [vLLM Semantic Router](https://app.vllm-sr.ai/docs) (System Duo). The [API docs](https://app.vllm-sr.ai/docs) set the origin to `https://app.vllm-sr.ai` and the System Two OpenAI base URL to `https://app.vllm-sr.ai/v1`. They document `POST /v1/chat/completions` with model `vllm-sr/auto`. Examples and the OpenAI SDK snippet send `Authorization: Bearer`. The same page also accepts `X-API-Key`. Arca uses Chat Completions on `https://app.vllm-sr.ai/v1` (that base plus `/chat/completions`). The docs do not describe an OpenAI Responses API, an Anthropic Messages API, or a Gemini generateContent contract, so none is stored. System One (`POST /v1/systemone` and `POST /v1/decisions`) returns calibrated decision probabilities rather than chat text, so it is not an Arca chat protocol and is not stored. This template is separate from the local `vllm` provider at `http://localhost:8000/v1`.

Probes on 2026-11-11: `POST /v1/chat/completions` with no key returns HTTP 401 `missing_api_key` (`Send your API key as Authorization: Bearer <key>. Get a free key at https://app.vllm-sr.ai`). A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `X-API-Key` on that route return HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key`. `POST /v1/responses` and `POST /v1/messages` with no key return that same missing-key 401. A bogus Bearer key on Responses and a bogus `X-API-Key` on Messages return `invalid_api_key`. `GET /v1/nonexistent` returns the same 401 with no key and with a bogus Bearer key, so a credential is checked before an unknown `/v1` path and these statuses do not show a Responses or Messages route. `POST /v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent` returns the website 404 (`Page not found`). Gemini is not stored. The System Two error table says a model other than `vllm-sr/auto` is HTTP 403 `model_not_allowed`. Authentication fails before that check, so this probe did not observe the 403.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` returns HTTP 200 `{object: "list", data: [...], models: [...]}`. A bogus Bearer key returns the same public list. On this date the body was 2,335 bytes with 11 `data` entries and no continuation field. Every `data` entry had `id`, `object: "model"`, and `owned_by: "vllm-sr"`. None had `name` or `context_length`. Ten entries are System One decision models and aliases: `vllm-sr/d3-ultra` (aliases `vllm-sr/d3-turbo`, `vllm-sr/d3`), `vllm-sr/d3-flash` (aliases `vllm-sr/d3-mini`, `vllm-sr/d3-nano`, `vllm-sr/d3-lite`, `vllm-sr/d3-edge`), `vllm-sr/vela3-turbo`, and `vllm-sr/vela3-flash`. Only `vllm-sr/auto` sets `"system": "two"`. The sibling `models` array has 10 System One rows keyed by `name` and does not include `vllm-sr/auto`. The docs say `data` lists System One's models and System Two's `vllm-sr/auto`, and that `models` stays System One's list. The generic OpenAI decoder reads every `data[].id`, so it would import the decision models. No reviewed HTTP discovery spec is registered, and the generic OpenAI `/v1/models` fallback is not used. With or without a credential, Arca does not call the network. The seed is only `vllm-sr/auto`. The docs and [System Two info](https://app.vllm-sr.ai/pt/api/info) give that id no separate display name and no context length, so the seed stores neither. `routes_to` on that date was `zai/glm-5.3-flash`, `qwen/qwen3.8-flash-next`, and `qwen/qwen3.8-27b`. Those are pool backends behind `vllm-sr/auto`, and the docs say any other model id is refused, so they are not seeded. An explicit Models list URL on a connection still uses the OpenAI catalog decoder.

The docs say the free APIs are for research and evaluation only and that commercial use is not permitted. A key is created on the home page in one click, with no account. Keys from this site use the prefix `sk-vsr-`. Live per-key limits from `GET /pt/api/info` on this date are 180 requests per minute, 600,000 tokens per minute, 16 concurrent requests, 108,000 requests per day, and 360,000,000 tokens per day. Daily quotas reset at 00:00 UTC. The catalog does not encode that quota.

Only `app.vllm-sr.ai` is mapped for host inference. `POST https://decision.vllm-sr.ai/v1/chat/completions` returns HTTP 404 `not_found` (`type: systemone_error`, `no such endpoint`). `GET https://decision.vllm-sr.ai/v1/models` returns HTTP 200 with the ten System One ids and does not include `vllm-sr/auto`. `vllm-sr.ai` and `www.vllm-sr.ai` are the marketing site; `GET /v1/models` there is the site 404. `mom.vllm-sr.ai` and `pytorch.vllm-sr.ai` answered `POST /v1/chat/completions` with the same missing-key 401 and tell the caller to get a key at `https://app.vllm-sr.ai`. The published System Two base is `app.vllm-sr.ai`, so those hosts stay `custom`. `docs.vllm-sr.ai` did not resolve. `api.vllm-sr.ai` failed the TLS handshake. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. The missing-key error publishes <https://app.vllm-sr.ai>. The template portal is that URL with kind `api-key`. An unsigned GET returns the app shell (title `System Duo · Thinking, fast and slow.`).

## Sail Research — 2026-11-10

`sailresearch` / `Sail Research` is a gateway Provider for [Sail](https://docs.sailresearch.com/). The [overview](https://docs.sailresearch.com/) and [quickstart](https://docs.sailresearch.com/quickstart) set the OpenAI-compatible base URL to `https://api.sailresearch.com/v1`. They document `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `POST /v1/responses`. The Anthropic examples set the SDK base URL to `https://api.sailresearch.com` and call `POST /v1/messages` with `x-api-key`. The published [OpenAPI](https://docs.sailresearch.com/openapi.json) uses server `https://api.sailresearch.com/v1` and lists `GET /models`, `POST /chat/completions`, `POST /responses`, and `POST /messages`. Chat Completions and Responses use `Authorization: Bearer`. Messages accepts that bearer scheme or `x-api-key` (`AnthropicApiKey`). Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://api.sailresearch.com/v1`, stores Responses on that same base URL, and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://api.sailresearch.com`. No Gemini format is documented.

Probes on 2026-11-10: `GET /v1/models` with no key returns HTTP 401 `authentication_error` (`Missing or invalid Authorization header`). A bogus Bearer key on that route returns HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key` (`Invalid API key`). A bogus `x-api-key` on `GET /v1/models` and on `POST /v1/chat/completions` still returns the missing-Authorization 401, so those routes do not treat `x-api-key` as the credential. `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `POST /v1/responses` with no key return the same missing-Authorization 401. A bogus Bearer key on both returns `invalid_api_key`. `POST /v1/responses` with `store: true` and with `store: false` both stop at that 401 before a body is checked. [API support](https://docs.sailresearch.com/support) says `store: false` is accepted for OpenAI compatibility and does not change Sail's temporary request storage, and it does not require that value, so no Responses storage override is declared. `POST /v1/messages` with no key returns HTTP 401 `authentication_error`. A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` on that route both return `Invalid API key`, so Arca's Messages `x-api-key` header is accepted as a credential. `POST /messages` without `/v1`, `GET /models` without `/v1`, `GET /v1/nonexistent`, `POST /v1beta/models/gemini:generateContent`, `GET /v1beta/models`, and `POST /v1/models/zai-org/GLM-5.3:generateContent` return HTTP 404 `invalid_request_error` (`Unknown endpoint`). Gemini is not stored. `GET https://api.sailresearch.com/openapi.json` is that same 404. The OpenAPI document is on the docs host.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` does not return a catalog. The OpenAPI describes the authenticated list as `{object: "list", data: [{id, object: "model", created, owned_by}]}` and does not include a context field. The live authenticated body was not observed. When a credential is configured, model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder on `GET /v1/models`. With no credential, Arca does not call the network and seeds the current ids from the [models](https://docs.sailresearch.com/models) page, in that page's order: `moonshotai/Kimi-K3.1-Pro` (Kimi K3.1 Pro), `moonshotai/Kimi-K3` (Kimi K3), `zai-org/GLM-5.3` (GLM-5.3), `zai-org/GLM-5.3-Flash` (GLM-5.3-Flash), `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017` (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 1017), `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813` (DeepSeek V4 Pro 0813), `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731` (DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731), `google/gemma-4-31B-it` (Gemma 4 31B IT), `nvidia/Gemma-4-31B-IT-NVFP4` (Gemma 4 31B IT (NVFP4)), `google/gemma-4-12B-it` (Gemma 4 12B IT), and `Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B` (Qwen3.6 35B A3B). The same page is the context source. It publishes `1M` for the first seven, `256K` for Gemma 4 31B IT, `262K` for the NVFP4 Gemma and for Qwen3.6 35B A3B, and `16K` for Gemma 4 12B IT. It does not publish integer token counts. The seed stores the decimal reading of those labels: 1,000,000, 256,000, 262,000, and 16,000. `Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B` is listed under flex-only models. The [pricing](https://docs.sailresearch.com/pricing) page lists the same eleven ids and no others. Pricing rates are per completion window and are not stored on the catalog.

The [pricing plans](https://docs.sailresearch.com/pricing) call the self-serve plan free to start and pay-as-you-go: `$5/month in free credits when you attach a payment method`, then usage-based prepaid credits. The [homepage](https://www.sailresearch.com/) and the [sign-up](https://app.sailresearch.com/sign-up) page say the same `$5 in monthly free credits`. [Rate limits](https://docs.sailresearch.com/rate-limits) says accounts using only promotional sign-up credits are limited to a smaller number of requests per minute, and that purchasing credits removes that limit. Those pages do not state a dollar amount for the promotional sign-up credits. The catalog has no free/paid flag, so this credit balance is not encoded and no model is marked free.

Only `api.sailresearch.com` is mapped for host inference. `www.sailresearch.com` and `sailresearch.com` are the marketing site. `docs.sailresearch.com` is the documentation site. `app.sailresearch.com` is the account app. `www.api.sailresearch.com` and `www.docs.sailresearch.com` did not resolve. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. The template portal is <https://app.sailresearch.com/sign-up> with kind `console`. An unsigned GET of that path returns the sign-up shell.

## EXAONE API Platform — 2026-11-09

`exaone` / `EXAONE API Platform` is the official Provider for LG AI Research's EXAONE API Platform. The [quick start](https://docs.exaone.ai/en/docs/quick-start) sets the base URL for every API request to `https://api.exaone.ai` and says each request sends the API key as `Authorization: Bearer`. The [K-EXAONE 2.5 API](https://docs.exaone.ai/en/docs/k-exaone-2.5) page says the API is OpenAI-compatible and documents `POST /v1/chat/completions` with model id `k-exaone_v2.5`. The [agent guide](https://docs.exaone.ai/en/docs/agent-start) sets the OpenAI-compatible client base to `https://api.exaone.ai/v1` and `transport: chat_completions`. Arca stores Chat Completions on `https://api.exaone.ai/v1` (that base plus `/chat/completions`). No Responses, Anthropic Messages, or Gemini contract is documented, so none is stored.

K-EXAONE 2.5 is text-only. The API page says the maximum context length is 524,288 tokens, and that input tokens plus `max_tokens` (including reasoning) cannot exceed that limit. The [models](https://docs.exaone.ai/en/docs/models) page labels the same window 512K and lists 750B parameters with 31B active, Korean and English, and a January 2026 knowledge cutoff. The seed stores id `k-exaone_v2.5`, display name `K-EXAONE 2.5`, and context length 524288. [EXAONE Tabular](https://docs.exaone.ai/en/docs/exaone-tabular) is a separate multipart prediction API (`POST /v1/tabular/classification` and `POST /v1/tabular/regression`). It is not a chat model and is not seeded.

The docs do not publish a Models list. Probes on 2026-11-09: `GET /v1/models` with no key returns HTTP 401 `{"error":{"message":"invalid api key format","type":"authentication_error","code":"invalid_api_key"}}`. A bogus Bearer key on that route returns HTTP 401 `invalid api key` in the same OpenAI error envelope. `POST /v1/chat/completions` with no key, with a bogus Bearer key, and with a bogus `x-api-key` returns that same OpenAI envelope. `GET /models`, `GET /v1`, `GET /v1/nonexistent`, `POST /v1/responses`, `POST /v1/messages`, `POST /messages`, and `POST /v1beta/models/k-exaone_v2.5:generateContent` return the platform envelope `{"result_code":401,"description":"UnAuthorizedError,..."}` instead, including with a bogus Bearer key. Those paths are not treated as chat protocols. The authenticated `GET /v1/models` 200 body was not observed.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` does not return a catalog. With no credential, Arca does not call the network and seeds the documented chat model. When a credential is configured, model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder on `GET /v1/models`. That decoder reads `id`. The docs do not describe the list schema.

The [changelog](https://docs.exaone.ai/en/docs/changelog) for 2026-11-09 says the service is currently available free of charge and names K-EXAONE 2.5 (750B) and EXAONE Tabular. The Korean changelog says the same (`본 서비스는 현재 무료로 제공됩니다`). No end date is published. The [terms](https://docs.exaone.ai/en/terms/terms-of-service) (effective 2026-11-09; the Korean text prevails if the languages differ) grant use for non-commercial purposes and say an API key is valid for 30 days from issuance. They also say an account that does not log in for 90 days may be marked dormant and its key deactivated. The [rate limits](https://docs.exaone.ai/en/docs/rate-limits) page describes response headers and shows an example 429. It does not publish a numeric quota. The catalog has no free, commercial, or quota field, so none of that is encoded. Only `api.exaone.ai` is mapped for host inference. `docs.exaone.ai` is the documentation site. `id.exaone.ai` is the account host: the docs shell sets `accountUrl` to `https://id.exaone.ai/mypage`, and an unsigned GET of that path returns HTTP 307 to `/`, which returns HTTP 307 to `/login` (title `EXAONE ID - Auth 관리`). `GET /api-keys`, `/keys`, and `/mypage/api-keys` on that host return HTTP 404, so no direct key-list URL is published. `showroom.exaone.ai` and `asset-showroom.exaone.ai` are the showroom and its image host. `console.exaone.ai`, `platform.exaone.ai`, and `app.exaone.ai` did not resolve. `exaone.ai` and `www.exaone.ai` did not complete a TLS handshake. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. The template portal is <https://id.exaone.ai/mypage> with kind `console`.

## IFM — 2026-11-08

`ifm` / `IFM` is the official Provider for the [Institute of Foundation Models](https://docs.ifm.ai/) (IFM) at MBZUAI. The [quickstart](https://docs.ifm.ai/docs/quickstart.md) sets `IFM_BASE_URL` to `https://api.ifm.ai/v1` and calls `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`. The [chat completions](https://docs.ifm.ai/docs/chat-completions.md) page documents that same URL. The quickstart example key is `IFM-xf...`. The OpenCode, OpenClaw, Pi, and Hermes guides use the example `sk-ifm-...`. Arca stores Chat Completions on `https://api.ifm.ai/v1` (that base plus `/chat/completions`). Chat Completions stays the default: it is the documented primary API, and Responses is not documented for every hosted model.

The published [docs index](https://docs.ifm.ai/llms.txt) lists Chat Completions and the Transcriptions API. It does not list an OpenAI Responses reference, an Anthropic Messages API, or a Gemini route. The public model catalog advertises `supported_endpoints` `/v1/responses` on `IFM/K2-Horizon-375B-A23B` only. `POST /v1/responses` is a live authenticated route, so Arca stores Responses on the same base and does not declare a storage override. The [Hermes](https://docs.ifm.ai/docs/hermes.md) guide mentions the Responses API only for a self-host whitespace plugin that also covers vLLM. No Anthropic-compatible base URL is documented, so Anthropic Messages is not stored. Gemini is not stored. Transcriptions (`POST /v1/audio/transcriptions`) are outside Arca chat models and are not stored.

Probes on 2026-11-08: `GET /v1/models` with no key, with an empty Bearer token, with a bogus Bearer key, with user agent `arca-provider-model-discovery`, and with `Python-urllib/3.12` each return HTTP 200 and the same 599-byte body. `POST /v1/chat/completions` with no key returns HTTP 401 `invalid_request_error` (`ifm-auth-error: missing_api_key`) and tells the caller to send `Authorization: Bearer`. A bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key` and points at `https://ifm.ai/docs/quick-start`. A bogus `x-api-key` on that route returns the missing-key 401, so the header is not accepted as a credential. An empty JSON body with a bogus Bearer key returns that same invalid-key 401. `POST /v1/responses` with no key returns the missing-key 401. A bogus Bearer key with `store: false` and with `store: true` returns the invalid-key 401 before a body is checked. The same invalid-key 401 is returned for model `IFM/Jais-3-MoVA-36B-A4B` on that route, so this probe does not show whether Jais 3 accepts Responses. `POST /v1/audio/transcriptions` with no key returns the missing-key 401. `POST /v1/messages` with no key returns that same OpenAI missing-key envelope, not an Anthropic error. A bogus `x-api-key` with `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01` is still the missing-key 401. A bogus Bearer key on `/v1/messages` is the invalid-key 401. `POST /messages`, `POST /anthropic/v1/messages`, `POST /v1/embeddings`, `POST /v1/completions`, `POST /v1/not-a-route`, `POST /v1beta/models/IFM/K2-Horizon-375B-A23B:generateContent`, and `POST /v1/models/IFM/K2-Horizon-375B-A23B:generateContent` return HTTP 404 `{"error_msg":"404 Route Not Found"}`. `GET /v1/nonexistent`, `GET /`, `GET /v1`, `GET /openapi.json`, and `GET /v1/openapi.json` return that same 404. `GET /v1/models/` with a trailing slash is that 404.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential. Anonymous `GET /v1/models` is the catalog, so no ids are hard-coded. On this date the body was `{object: "list", data: [three entries]}` and had no continuation field. Every entry had `id`, `object: "model"`, `owned_by: "MBZUAI"`, and `display_name`. The decoder reads `id`, `display_name`, and `context_length`. It does not read `max_model_len`. `IFM/K2-Horizon-375B-A23B` (display name `K2 Horizon 375B A23B`) has `context_length` and `max_model_len` both `524288`, and `supported_endpoints` `["/v1/chat/completions", "/v1/responses"]`, so the discovered summary carries context length 524288. `IFM/Jais-3-MoVA-36B-A4B` (display name `Jais 3 MoVA 36B A4B`) lists only `/v1/chat/completions` and has no `context_length`. The [model overview](https://docs.ifm.ai/docs/model-catalog.md) says that model has a 512K context; the live list does not, so the discovered summary has no context length. `IFM/Jais-ASR` (display name `Jais ASR`) lists only `/v1/audio/transcriptions`. Rows whose `owned_by` is `MBZUAI`, whose `id` starts with `IFM/`, and whose `supported_endpoints` array contains neither `/v1/chat/completions` nor `/v1/responses` are dropped. Other providers' rows are not filtered by that field. A saved credential sends `Authorization: Bearer` to the same URL. The authenticated 200 body for a valid key was not observed.

The [limits](https://docs.ifm.ai/docs/limits.md) page says every API key gets 10,000,000 tokens per day, counting prompt and completion tokens together, resetting at 00:00 UTC. Requests past the cap return `429` with `rate_limit_error`. The page does not call the allowance free, does not mention a credit card, and does not publish a price. A public summary at [tahoo.tech](https://tahoo.tech/resources/ifm-k2-horizon-free-api-10m-tokens-daily) (last verified Nov 1, 2026) calls the same cap a free tier and says it resets at 19:00 ET. 00:00 UTC is 19:00 in US Eastern Standard Time and 20:00 in Eastern Daylight Time. The docs state 00:00 UTC. The provider template has no free flag, and discovered summaries keep `id`, `name`, and `context_length` only, so the cap is not encoded.

Only `api.ifm.ai` is mapped for host inference. `docs.ifm.ai` serves the documentation site. `ifm.ai` and `platform.ifm.ai` answer with a Cloudflare challenge (HTTP 403, title `Just a moment...`). `www.ifm.ai` is HTTP 301 to `https://ifm.ai/`. `ifm.mbzuai.ac.ae` is HTTP 301 to `https://ifm.ai/`. `console.ifm.ai` and `chat.ifm.ai` resolve and return that same challenge; `GET https://chat.ifm.ai/v1/models` is HTTP 403. `app.ifm.ai`, `www.api.ifm.ai`, `api.ifm.com`, `status.ifm.ai`, and `dashboard.ifm.ai` did not resolve. `mbzuai.ac.ae` is the university site. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. The invalid-key error links `https://ifm.ai/docs/quick-start`, which redirects to `https://docs.ifm.ai/quick-start`, and that path is the docs 404. The working quickstart is `https://docs.ifm.ai/docs/quickstart.md`. Keys are created at the URL named in that quickstart. Unsigned `GET https://platform.ifm.ai/api-keys` is the Cloudflare challenge, not an app shell. The template portal is <https://platform.ifm.ai/api-keys> with kind `api-key`. The icon is the favicon mark from `https://ifm.ai/favicon.ico`.

## OptraCloud — 2026-11-08

`optracloud` / `OptraCloud` is a gateway Provider for [OptraCloud](https://optracloud.com/) (Globussoft Technologies Pvt. Ltd., CIN U72300CT2012PTC000452). The homepage says “OptraCloud is the AI infrastructure arm of Globussoft Technologies, incorporated 2012.” The [API docs](https://optracloud.com/docs.html) set `OPENAI_BASE_URL` to `https://api.optracloud.com/v1` and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to `https://api.optracloud.com`. They document `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer` and `POST /v1/messages` with `x-api-key` plus `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`. The Claude Code example sends the same key as `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`. Keys look like `sk-optra-…` and are created at `/portal/apikeys`. The published [llms.txt](https://api.optracloud.com/llms.txt) lists `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/messages`, `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens`, and `GET /v1/models`. It does not list an OpenAI Responses API or a Gemini route. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://api.optracloud.com/v1` and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://api.optracloud.com`, so the Messages client base stays `https://api.optracloud.com` and the path is `/v1/messages`. Token counting is outside Arca chat models and is not stored. Responses and Gemini are not stored. llms.txt links docs at `https://optracloud.com/docs`, and that path returns the website 404. The working page is `https://optracloud.com/docs.html`.

Probes on 2026-11-08: `GET /v1/models` with no key, with an empty Bearer token, with a bogus Bearer key, and with user agent `arca-provider-model-discovery` each return HTTP 200 and the same 422-byte body. `POST /v1/chat/completions` with no key returns HTTP 401 `authentication_error` (`No API key provided. Send 'Authorization: Bearer sk-optra-...' or 'x-api-key: sk-optra-...'.`). A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` on that route return HTTP 401 `authentication_error` (`Invalid API key, or the key has been revoked.`). An empty body and a missing body with a bogus Bearer key return that same invalid-key 401. `POST /v1/messages` with `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01` and no key returns HTTP 401 in the Anthropic error envelope with the same missing-key sentence. A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` on that route return HTTP 401 in that envelope with the invalid-key sentence, so Arca's Messages `x-api-key` header is accepted as a credential. `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens` with no key and with a bogus `x-api-key` returns those same 401s. `POST /v1/responses` with no key, with a bogus Bearer key and `store: false`, and with `store: true` returns HTTP 404 `invalid_request_error` (`No such endpoint: /v1/responses`). `POST /v1/embeddings` and `POST /v1/images/generations` return that same 404 shape. `GET /v1/nonexistent` and `GET /v1/openapi.json` return HTTP 404 `No such endpoint` with or without a bogus Bearer key. `POST /v1/models/optra-coder:generateContent` returns HTTP 404 `No such endpoint: /v1/models/optra-coder:generateContent`. `GET /`, `GET /v1`, `GET /models`, `GET /openapi.json`, `POST /messages`, `POST /v1beta/models/optra-coder:generateContent`, `GET /v1beta/models`, and `POST /v1beta/interactions` return HTTP 303 to `/portal/login`. A `Python-urllib/3.12` user agent on `GET /v1/models` and `POST /v1/chat/completions` returns HTTP 403 `error code: 1010`, which matches the docs troubleshooting row for that client.

The docs say to use the model id `optra-coder`. They say it is what `/v1/models` advertises, and that `qwen3-coder` and `qwen3-coder-30b` are accepted aliases. Anonymous `GET /v1/models` returned `{object: "list", data: [one entry]}` and no continuation field. The entry is `id` `optra-coder`, `object` `model`, `owned_by` `optracloud`, `context_window` `131072`, `description` `Qwen3.8-27B. Agentic coding, tool use, image input.`, `category` `chat`, `input_modalities` `["text", "image"]`, `output_modalities` `["text"]`, `max_output_tokens` `8192`, `supports_tools` true, `supports_streaming` true, `billing` `plan`, and `pricing_url` `https://optracloud.com/`. It has no `name`. The aliases and the string `Qwen3.8-27B` are not ids in that list. A bogus Bearer key on `POST /v1/chat/completions` returned the same invalid-key 401 for `optra-coder`, `qwen3-coder`, `qwen3-coder-30b`, `qwen3.8-27b`, `Qwen3.8-27B`, and `not-a-model`, so this probe does not show whether an alias is accepted. The same `GET /v1/models` with `x-api-key` returned a different body: `{data: [{type: "model", id: "optra-coder", display_name: "Qwen3.8-27B. Agentic coding, tool use, image input.", created_at: "2026-01-01T00:00:00Z"}], has_more: false, first_id: "optra-coder", last_id: "optra-coder"}`. llms.txt describes the one model as `optra-coder: Qwen3.8-27B. Agentic coding, tool use, image input. Context 131072 tokens, max output 8192, input text+image, tools and streaming supported.`

The homepage card heading is `Qwen3.8-27B`, with the sentence “Alibaba’s newest open model, released August 2026, on our own hardware in India at a 128K context — and it reads images, not just text.” A savings-table row labels `Qwen3.8-27B` as `serving now · 128K context`. A dedicated-card row says `Up to 32B — incl. Qwen3.8-27B` at context `256K`. The docs say shared plans cap at 128K per request and that dedicated cards carry 256K to a full 1M. [free.html](https://optracloud.com/free.html) says “Published output-token prices for Qwen3.8-27B, the exact model we serve”. The homepage JavaScript `MODELS` array has no row named `Qwen3.8-27B`. A separate homepage snippet sets `base_url` to `https://<you>.optracloud.com/v1` and `model` to `deepseek-r1-distill-qwen-14b`. `you.optracloud.com` did not resolve. The comparison table has a column titled `Self-hosting` beside `OptraCloud`. The site says the served weights run “on our own hardware”, that speeds are “measured on our own hardware with vLLM and 4-bit weights”, and the footer says “dedicated GPUs and open-weight models”.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential. Anonymous `GET /v1/models` is the catalog, so no ids are hard-coded and the documented aliases are not seeded. The decoder reads `id`. It reads `display_name` or `name` for the summary name, and `context_window` for the context length. The anonymous body has `id` and `context_window` `131072` and no `name` or `display_name`, so the discovered summary is `optra-coder` with no name and context length 131072. `description`, `max_output_tokens`, and `billing` are not copied. A saved credential sends `Authorization: Bearer` to the same URL. The OpenAI decoder does not follow `has_more`. There is one entry and no continuation field on the anonymous body.

The docs say “New accounts get 150,000,000 free output tokens.” They say “We bill generated tokens. The context you send is free”. HTTP 402 is documented as “Free grant exhausted” with the action “Invite someone for another 150,000,000, or start a plan”. The homepage free section says “Verify an email and a mobile number and you get 150,000,000 output tokens free” and “No credit card, no trial clock.” The same page’s hero says “Verify a mobile number and start generating — no credit card, no trial clock.” A note on that page says “A verified mobile number is required — it is what stops one person collecting the grant a hundred times, and it is the only thing we ask for.” [free.html](https://optracloud.com/free.html) says “Verify an email and a mobile number and start generating. No credit card, no trial clock, and no meter running while you think.” It also says “A verified mobile is the only thing standing between this offer and somebody collecting it a hundred times. It is also the only personal detail we ask for” and “No card on file means no surprise charge.” The live join page [https://optracloud.com/portal/join](https://optracloud.com/portal/join) (title `Get free tokens — OptraCloud`) says “No card — just a mobile number, so we know you are real.” Its form is “Continue with Google” or a required mobile number, a six-digit code, and an optional invite code. It has no email field. The homepage and free page say another 150,000,000 tokens is granted to both people after the invited person generates 100,000 tokens. The docs, free page, homepage free section, and join page print no end date. The free tier is paced to 60 token/sec on the docs, the homepage, free.html, and the join page. The docs and the homepage say paid plans hold a 60 token/sec floor. The join page says “paid plans start at 100”. The provider template has no free flag. Discovered summaries keep `id`, `name`, and `context_length` only. The catalog `billing` value `plan` is not encoded.

Only `api.optracloud.com` is mapped for host inference. `optracloud.com` and `www.optracloud.com` serve the marketing site. `GET https://www.optracloud.com/v1/models` is HTTP 301 to `https://optracloud.com/v1/models`, and that path is the website 404. `portal.optracloud.com` returns HTTP 526 `error code: 526`. `docs.optracloud.com`, `app.optracloud.com`, `www.api.optracloud.com`, `you.optracloud.com`, `api.optracloud.ai`, and `optracloud.ai` did not resolve. `globussoft.com` and `www.globussoft.com` resolve and stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. Unsigned `GET https://optracloud.com/portal/apikeys` and `GET https://api.optracloud.com/portal/apikeys` are HTTP 303 to `/portal/login`. `GET /portal/login` is HTTP 200 (title `Sign in — OptraCloud`). `GET /portal/signup` is HTTP 303 to `/portal/join`. The template portal is <https://optracloud.com/portal/apikeys> with kind `api-key`.

## FreeInference — 2026-11-08

`freeinference` / `FreeInference` is a gateway Provider for [FreeInference](https://freeinference.org/) (Harvard SEAS, MadSys Lab). The [quickstart](https://doc.freeinference.org/quickstart) and [integrations](https://doc.freeinference.org/integrations) set the OpenAI-compatible base URL to `https://freeinference.org/v1`. They document `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`. The [API headers](https://doc.freeinference.org/api_headers) page also accepts the same key as `X-API-Key` without the `Bearer` prefix. Keys use the prefix `hyi-`. Arca stores Chat Completions on `https://freeinference.org/v1`.

The [Claude Code](https://doc.freeinference.org/claude-code) guide sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to `https://freeinference.org/anthropic` and says the client appends `/v1/messages`. It sends the same key through `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`. Arca stores Anthropic Messages at `https://freeinference.org/anthropic`, so the path is `/v1/messages`. The [batch](https://doc.freeinference.org/batches) guide names `POST /v1/responses` as a direct endpoint the gateway runs itself, and the homepage build `7f7585b` links a deployed [Responses design](https://github.com/HarvardMadSys/hybridInference/blob/7f7585bc60a303f9dc8e2a0f219ab7f0b99f3255/docs/agents/specs/2026-06-23-openai-responses-api-design.md) whose status is Implemented. That spec documents `POST /v1/responses` with `store` defaulting to `true`, plus retrieve and delete. The batch guide requires an independent batched Responses request to omit `store` or set it to `false`; it does not say the direct route rejects `store: true`. Arca stores Responses on `https://freeinference.org/v1` and does not declare a storage override. No Gemini generateContent or Interactions contract is published on [doc.freeinference.org](https://doc.freeinference.org/). The deployed source has an upstream Gemini adapter that translates OpenAI chat into Google's API. That is not a client route, so Gemini is not stored.

Probes on 2026-11-08: `GET /v1/models` with no key, with a bogus Bearer key, and with a bogus `X-API-Key` each return HTTP 200 `{object: "list", data: [...]}` and the same 1,558-byte body. `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/responses`, and `POST /v1/embeddings` with no key return HTTP 401 (`Missing API key. Use 'Authorization: Bearer hyi-xxx' or 'X-API-Key: hyi-xxx'`). A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `X-API-Key` on chat completions return HTTP 401 `Invalid or expired API key`. `POST /v1/responses` with `store: true` and with `store: false` both stop at that invalid-key 401 before a body is checked. `POST /anthropic/v1/messages` with no key returns HTTP 401 in the Anthropic error envelope (`authentication_error`, the same missing-key text). A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` return HTTP 401 `Invalid or expired API key`, with or without `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, so Arca's Messages `x-api-key` header is accepted as a credential. `POST /v1/messages` returns that same Anthropic envelope. The documented base is still `https://freeinference.org/anthropic`. `POST /messages` without `/v1` is the website HTML 404. `POST /anthropic/messages` is HTTP 404 `not_found_error`. `GET /v1/nonexistent` and `POST /v1/models/glm-5.3-flash:generateContent` are HTTP 404 `Not Found` with or without a bogus Bearer key. `POST /v1/interactions` is that same API 404. `POST /v1beta/models/glm-5.3-flash:generateContent`, `POST /v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent`, `GET /v1beta/models`, and `POST /v1beta/interactions` are the website HTML 404. `GET /models`, `GET /openapi.json`, and `POST /v1/openapi.json` are not an API catalog. Gemini is not stored.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential. [Available models](https://doc.freeinference.org/models) says the authenticated `GET /v1/models` list is the source of truth and is filtered by access level. On this date the anonymous body had no continuation field and three `data` entries, each with `id`, `name`, and `object: "model"`. `context_length` was an integer on every entry, so discovered models carry a context length. The decoder reads `id`, `name`, and `context_length`. It does not read `output_modalities`, so it does not drop the embedding row. No model ids are hard-coded. The three rows were `qwen3.6-35b` (name `Qwen3.6 35B (high speed)`, context `262144`, output `text`), `deepseek-v4-flash` (name `DeepSeek V4 Flash (high speed)`, context `1000000`, output `text`), and `bge-m3` (name `BGE-M3`, context `8192`, output `embedding`). A bogus key returned that same list, not HTTP 401. The authenticated 200 body for a valid key was not observed. `GET /anthropic/v1/models` is a different document (`data: [{type: "model", id, display_name}]`, `has_more: false`) and is not the discovery route. On this date it listed only `qwen3.6-35b` and `deepseek-v4-flash`, with or without a key and with or without `anthropic-version`. Sending `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01` to `GET /v1/models` returns that same Anthropic shape. Arca does not send that header.

The models page also lists `glm-5.3-flash`, `minimax-m3`, `glm-5.2` (Pro), and `kimi-k2.7-code` (Pro). None of those ids were in the anonymous list. The homepage curl example `glm-5.1` was not on the models page or in that list. Those ids are not seeded. The models page says `bge-m3` is an embedding model for `/v1/embeddings` and is not a chat model. It still appears in the public OpenAI list, and the shared decoder keeps it. `POST /v1/embeddings` is authenticated and is not an Arca chat protocol.

The homepage says the service is free for research, that no credit card is required, and that the quota is generous. It does not print a token count. The models page marks an access level (`Free` or `Pro`) rather than a user price. The anonymous catalog includes a `pricing` object. For `deepseek-v4-flash` the prompt, completion, and cache-read strings are `0.44`, `1.32`, and `0.014`. The models page says those DeepSeek figures are upstream reference rates used for internal accounting, not fees charged to users. The catalog has no free/paid flag, and discovered summaries keep `id`, `name`, and `context_length` only, so this is not encoded.

Keys are created after signup. The deployed signup page shows: "Open to Harvard students — sign up with your @harvard.edu email for instant access. Everyone else: please describe your use case below — we review and approve manually." The signed-out page config sets `fastTrackOrg` to `Harvard` and `fastTrackDomain` to `harvard.edu`. A submitted registration can come back with `requires_approval`. The homepage and the same config say: "All prompts and responses are logged for research purposes. Anonymized data derived from requests may be open-sourced to support research." The [terms](https://freeinference.org/terms) (updated 2026-06-20) say prompts and responses may be logged, that sanitization does not guarantee removal of sensitive data, and: "Do not submit sensitive personal information, confidential information, regulated data, secrets, credentials, or data you are not authorized to process through the service." The provider template has no notes field, so those two caveats are recorded here. The template portal is <https://freeinference.org/signup> with kind `console`. After an account exists, keys are managed at <https://freeinference.org/dashboard/keys>. An unsigned GET of that path returns the app shell (title `FreeInference`). `GET /keys` and `GET /api-keys` are the website 404. `logoUrl` and `faviconUrl` in that config are empty, and `GET /favicon.ico`, `/favicon.svg`, `/icon.svg`, and `/apple-touch-icon.png` are the website 404, so no icon is bundled.

Only `freeinference.org` is mapped for host inference. The site and the API share that host. `doc.freeinference.org` is the documentation site. `www.freeinference.org` and `api.freeinference.org` answered HTTP 521. `agents.freeinference.org` is the Cloud Agent site, `status.freeinference.org` is status, `openinfra.freeinference.org` is the OpenInfra dashboard, and `data.freeinference.org` serves research data. `docs.freeinference.org` did not resolve. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. The homepage footer names the deployed source `HarvardMadSys/hybridInference` at `7f7585b`.

## Nodecode — 2026-11-08

`nodecode` / `Nodecode` is a gateway Provider for [Nodecode](https://nodecode.ai/docs/models). [Models and providers](https://nodecode.ai/docs/models) says nodecode.ai serves its own models at `https://api.nodecode.ai/v1`, on OpenAI's wire. The same page lists `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/responses` (OpenAI's Responses API), `POST /v1/messages` and `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens` (Anthropic's Messages API), `POST /v1/images/generations`, `GET /v1/models`, `GET /v1/models/ID`, and `GET /v1/usage`. The [pricing page](https://nodecode.ai/pricing) prints the base URL `https://api.nodecode.ai/v1`. The Claude Code example sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to `https://api.nodecode.ai` and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` to an `sk-nc-` key. The Codex example sets `base_url` to `https://api.nodecode.ai/v1`. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://api.nodecode.ai/v1`, stores Responses on that same base, and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://api.nodecode.ai`. No Gemini generateContent contract is documented, so Gemini is not stored. Image generation is not an Arca chat protocol and is not stored.

Probes on 2026-11-08: `GET /v1/models`, `GET /v1/models/nodecode-cyber-v1`, `GET /v1/usage`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, and `POST /v1/responses` with no key return HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key` (`Missing API key. Pass it as Authorization: Bearer sk-nc-...`), in the OpenAI error envelope. A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` on `GET /v1/models` and `POST /v1/chat/completions` return HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key` (`Invalid API key. Keys start with sk-nc-...`), so both headers are read as credentials. `POST /v1/responses` with no model, with `store: true`, and with `store: false` all stop at that 401 before a body is checked. The docs do not require `store: false`, so no Responses storage override is declared. `POST /v1/messages` with no key returns HTTP 401 in the Anthropic error envelope (`{"type":"error","error":{...}}`). A bogus `x-api-key` and a bogus Bearer key, each sent with `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, return the same invalid-key 401, so Arca's Messages `x-api-key` header is accepted as a credential. `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens` with a bogus `x-api-key` returns that same 401. `POST /messages` without `/v1` is HTTP 404 with an empty body, which matches the documented Anthropic base (the client appends `/v1/messages`). `GET /v1/nonexistent` is HTTP 404 with or without a bogus Bearer key. `GET /models`, `GET /v1/models/`, `GET /openapi.json`, and `GET /` on the API host are HTTP 404. `POST /v1beta/models/nodecode-cyber-v1:generateContent` is HTTP 404. `POST /v1/models/nodecode-cyber-v1:generateContent` is HTTP 405; `OPTIONS` on that path allows only `GET, HEAD`, and `GET` with a bogus Bearer key is the model-by-id 401, so the colon suffix is not a Gemini route. `POST /v1/images/generations` with a bogus Bearer key is HTTP 401, which confirms the documented image route and does not add a chat protocol.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` does not return a catalog. [Pricing](https://nodecode.ai/docs/pricing) says listing models costs nothing; the probe still requires a key. The authenticated 200 body was not observed, and no published page prints the list JSON. When a credential is configured, model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder on `GET /v1/models`, because the documented wire is OpenAI's. With no credential, Arca does not call the network. The seed is the one chat model a session can run on, from the models page: `nodecode-cyber-v1` (Cyber v1). [Pricing](https://nodecode.ai/docs/pricing) says its context window is 1,048,576 tokens (1M). The live pricing page says context length 1M and does not print that integer. `nodecode-image-v1` is the image model (`POST /v1/images/generations`, `b64_json`) and is not seeded. `nodecode-decide-v1` is the classifier (probabilities, never prose, 32K context on the models page) and is not offered as a session model, so it is not seeded. The CLI row `nodecode-free/nodecode-cyber-v1` is a provider/model pair inside Nodecode, not an API model id, and is not seeded.

The free key is not a general API grant, and nothing in this catalog is marked free. The models page says Cyber v1 is free from the Nodecode CLI only after the account has topped up at least $2 or €2, on model `nodecode-cyber-v1` alone, with a 256K window. Any other client, including Claude Code, Codex, and other code, answers 403 `free_needs_nodecode`. Another model answers 403 `model_not_free`. A key made at <https://nodecode.ai/account/keys> with Free key ticked has the same client limit. Arca is another client, so that key is not treated as usable here. The documented credential for Chat Completions, Responses, and Messages is a paid `sk-nc-` key. A successful paid response was not observed. The seeded context is the paid 1,048,576-token window, not the free-key 256K window.

The live [pricing page](https://nodecode.ai/pricing) and [docs pricing](https://nodecode.ai/docs/pricing) do not agree, so no price, concurrency, or top-up amount is encoded. On 2026-11-08 the page shows Cyber v1 cache-hit $0.0045 and $0.009, cache-miss $0.165 and $0.33, and output $0.66 and $1.32, and says Friday–Sunday Amsterdam time is 50% less, with the struck-through price returning on Monday. It says top up from $2, up to 12 requests at once, and that Nodecode itself is free while hosted models are paid per token. The docs page, updated 2026-11-06, prints Cyber v1 cached input 0.009, input 0.45, and output 1.80, says the price is the same at every hour, says an account runs 10 requests at once, and says a top-up is a whole amount from 5 to 10,000. It also says the prices are on the pricing page.

Only `api.nodecode.ai` is mapped for host inference. `nodecode.ai` is the site and stays `custom`, including `/docs/models`, `/pricing`, and `/account/keys`. `www.nodecode.ai` redirects to `https://nodecode.ai/`. `docs.nodecode.ai`, `app.nodecode.ai`, `dash.nodecode.ai`, `console.nodecode.ai`, and `account.nodecode.ai` resolve and return HTTP 404. `www.api.nodecode.ai` did not resolve. `api.nodecode.com` failed the TLS handshake (`unrecognized name`). `nodecode.com` and `www.nodecode.com` are a parked GoDaddy page. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. Keys start with `sk-nc-` and are created at <https://nodecode.ai/account/keys>. An unsigned GET of that path is HTTP 307 to `/login`; the response title is `API keys · NODECODE`. The template portal is that URL with kind `api-key`.

## xKiro — 2026-11-08

`xkiro` / `xKiro` is a gateway Provider for [xKiro](https://xkiro.com), a third-party AI gateway. [llms.txt](https://docs.xkiro.com/llms.txt) says one API reaches many models across several providers and speaks the OpenAI Chat Completions and Anthropic Messages wire formats. The [API overview](https://docs.xkiro.com/api/overview/) sets the server base to `https://api.xkiro.com` and documents `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `POST /v1/messages`. The [OpenAI SDK](https://docs.xkiro.com/guides/sdk-openai/) guide sets `base_url` to `https://api.xkiro.com/v1` because that SDK appends `/chat/completions`. The [Anthropic SDK](https://docs.xkiro.com/guides/sdk-anthropic/) guide and the [Claude Code](https://docs.xkiro.com/guides/tools/claude-code/) guide set the base to `https://api.xkiro.com` with no `/v1`, because those clients append `/v1/messages`. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://api.xkiro.com/v1` and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://api.xkiro.com`. [Authentication](https://docs.xkiro.com/guides/authentication/) says `Authorization: Bearer` and `x-api-key` are equivalent. Examples use the key prefix `sk-xt-`. The Claude Code guide sets `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` (sent as Bearer) or `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (sent as `x-api-key`) and says xKiro reads either header.

The overview endpoint table, [llms.txt](https://docs.xkiro.com/llms.txt), and the docs sitemap do not describe an OpenAI Responses API or a Gemini `generateContent` route. `https://docs.xkiro.com/api/responses/` is the documentation 404. Speech, image, web search, web fetch, and the remote MCP route are documented and are not Arca chat protocols. `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens` is documented and is not stored. Responses and Gemini are not stored.

Probes on 2026-11-08: `POST /v1/chat/completions` with no key returns HTTP 401 `authentication_error` (`Missing ClientApiKey. Send "Authorization: Bearer <key>" or the "x-api-key" header.`). A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` on that route return HTTP 401 `authentication_error` (`Invalid or disabled ClientApiKey.`). The authentication page's example invalid-key message is `Invalid API key.`; the live bogus key used the disabled-key wording above. `POST /v1/messages` with no key returns HTTP 401 in the Anthropic shape `{"type":"error","error":{"type":"authentication_error","message":"Missing ClientApiKey. ..."}}`. A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` (with `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`) on that route return the same Anthropic shape with `Invalid or disabled ClientApiKey.`, so Arca's Messages `x-api-key` header is accepted as a credential. `POST /messages` without `/v1` is the nginx 404. `POST /v1/v1/messages` is HTTP 404 `Cannot POST /v1/v1/messages`. `POST /v1/responses` with no key, and the same call with `store: true` and with `store: false`, returns the chat missing-key 401 before a body is checked. A bogus Bearer key on `/v1/responses` returns the chat invalid-key 401. `POST /responses` without `/v1` is the nginx 404. `POST /v1/nonexistent` is HTTP 404 `Cannot POST /v1/nonexistent`, so `/v1/responses` is a registered path rather than an unknown one. The published contract still does not describe Responses, and the body is not checked before auth, so Responses is not stored. `POST /v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent` and `POST /v1beta/models/google/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent` are the nginx 404 with or without `x-goog-api-key`. `POST /v1/models/google/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent` is HTTP 404 `Cannot POST` that path. Gemini is not stored. `GET /` and `GET /models` on the API host are the nginx 404. `GET /v1` is HTTP 404 `Cannot GET /v1`.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential. [Authentication](https://docs.xkiro.com/guides/authentication/) lists `GET /v1/models` as public. The overview calls that list the chat catalog and says speech and image models are not in it. Anonymous `GET /v1/models` on this date returned HTTP 200 `{object: "list", data: [...]}` with 69 entries, 29,604 bytes, and no continuation field. SHA-256 `e3e3f0f336cf4cd592590c40fc5070f90aa331e5dda9a6ce0fcb50af4944d175`. A bogus Bearer key returned HTTP 200 with that same digest. A bogus `x-api-key` also returned HTTP 200 and the same length. Every entry had `id`, `object: "model"`, `display_name`, and an integer `context_length` (8,192 through 1,050,000). None had `name`. Every `created` was `1`. Every `modality` was `chat`. The decoder reads `id`, `display_name` as the name, and `context_length`, so each discovered model carries a name and a context length. No model ids are hard-coded. `access_tier` was `free` on 26, `paid` on 29, and `premium` on 14. The 26 free rows are exactly the rows whose `pricing.input` and `pricing.output` are both `0`. Two paid rows set `min_plan_usd` to `100` and `min_plan_names` to `Ultra` and `Power`: `anthropic/claude-fable-5-1` and `openai/gpt-6-astra`. `owned_by` is a catalog label. The 16 `cohere/` ids and `sensenova/sensenova-6.8-flash-lite` use `owned_by` `api`. The decoder does not read tier, price, plan, or `owned_by`.

xKiro is a third-party reseller gateway. Model ids and prices are the values this gateway advertises. They do not verify an upstream source. The [homepage](https://xkiro.com/) says the free plan is 26 free models, no card, 500K tokens a day, and 1M after Telegram verification. Its plan comparison lists daily free-model tokens as Free 500K (1M after Telegram verification), Pro 8M, Pro+ 16M, Max 32M, Ultra 160M, and Power 320M, and it marks some plan prices "Up to −96%" against a higher struck price. For `openai/gpt-6.1-sol` the public catalog rate is input `2` and output `10` per 1M tokens, matching that page's struck pair, while the plan column shows `0.067` and `0.34`. The [signup page](https://xkiro.com/register) says "40+ free models". The Claude Code guide says "95+ models". The [pricing guide](https://docs.xkiro.com/guides/pricing/) describes a daily free-token allowance and does not print these counts. The catalog does not encode a quota.

Only `api.xkiro.com` is mapped for host inference. `GET https://xkiro.com/v1/models` returned the same 29,604-byte body and the same digest. `POST https://xkiro.com/v1/chat/completions`, `POST https://xkiro.com/v1/messages`, and `POST https://xkiro.com/v1/responses` with no key returned the same missing-key 401s as the API host. The published base is `api.xkiro.com`, and `xkiro.com` is the product site, so the apex host stays `custom`. `docs.xkiro.com` serves the documentation site, and `POST /v1/chat/completions` there is the docs 404. `www.xkiro.com`, `app.xkiro.com`, `dashboard.xkiro.com`, `console.xkiro.com`, `register.xkiro.com`, `portal.xkiro.com`, `chat.xkiro.com`, `status.xkiro.com`, `api.www.xkiro.com`, `www.api.xkiro.com`, `xkiro.ai`, and `api.xkiro.ai` did not resolve. `xtrouter.com` is HTTP 301 to `https://xkiro.com/j/xtrouter`, which is HTTP 307 to `https://xkiro.com/register`. `api.xtrouter.com` returned Cloudflare HTTP 522. `www.xtrouter.com` did not resolve. `cdn.xtrouter.com` is the marketing image host. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. Keys are created on the API Keys page. An unsigned GET of <https://xkiro.com/dashboard/api/keys> is HTTP 307 to `/login?next=%2Fdashboard%2Fapi%2Fkeys` (the login page title is `Sign in | xKiro`). The template portal is that keys URL with kind `api-key`. `GET https://xkiro.com/register` is HTTP 200 (title `Create account | xKiro`).

## Eden AI — 2026-11-08

`edenai` / `Eden AI` is a gateway Provider for [Eden AI](https://www.edenai.co/docs/). The [chat completions](https://www.edenai.co/docs/v3/llms/chat-completions) guide and the published [OpenAPI](https://api.edenai.run/v3/docs/openapi.json) set the OpenAI-compatible base to `https://api.edenai.run/v3` on server `https://api.edenai.run`. They document `POST /v3/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer` (`AuthBearer`). The OpenAI SDK examples use `base_url` `https://api.edenai.run/v3`. Arca stores Chat Completions on that base with `/chat/completions`. Model ids in the default catalog are `provider/model` endpoint ids. A name without a provider is a routable alias and is not a separate default-catalog id.

[Responses](https://www.edenai.co/docs/v3/llms/responses) documents `POST /v3/responses` on the same base. The OpenAPI lists that path, plus retrieve and delete. `store` defaults to `true`. The guide says to pass `store: false` to stay stateless, and it does not require that value or reject `store: true`. Arca stores Responses on `https://api.edenai.run/v3` and does not declare a storage override. [Create Anthropic Message](https://www.edenai.co/docs/api-reference/anthropic-messages/create-anthropic-message) documents `POST /v3/v1/messages` with the same bearer scheme. The [Claude Code](https://www.edenai.co/docs/v3/integrations/claude-code) guide sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to `https://api.edenai.run/v3` and says the client appends `/v1/messages`. It says `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` is sent as `Authorization: Bearer` and that `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` also works. Arca stores Anthropic Messages on that same base, so the client base stays `https://api.edenai.run/v3` and the path is `/v1/messages`. No Gemini generateContent contract is documented, so Gemini is not stored.

Probes on 2026-11-08: `GET /v3/models` with no key returns HTTP 200 `{object: "list", data: [...]}`. The OpenAPI marks that operation with no security, and [List LLM Models](https://www.edenai.co/docs/v3/llms/listing-models) says the endpoint is public. A bogus Bearer key on that route returns HTTP 401 `{"detail":"Invalid token"}`. `POST /v3/chat/completions`, `POST /v3/responses`, and `POST /v3/v1/messages` with no key return HTTP 403 `{"detail":"Not authenticated"}`. A bogus Bearer key on those three routes returns HTTP 401 `{"detail":"Invalid token"}`. A bogus `x-api-key` on `POST /v3/chat/completions` and `POST /v3/v1/messages` returns that same invalid-token 401, so Arca's Messages `x-api-key` header is accepted as a credential. `POST /v3/responses` with `store: true` and with `store: false` both stop at that 401 before a body is checked. `GET /v3/nonexistent` and `POST /v3/messages` return HTTP 404 `{"detail":"Not Found"}`. `GET /v1/models`, `GET /models`, `POST /v1/responses`, and `POST /v1/messages` return the website 404. `POST /v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent` returns the website 404. `POST /v3/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent` returns HTTP 404 `{"detail":"Not Found"}`.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential. With no credential, Arca calls `GET /v3/models` and does not seed ids. On this date the anonymous body was 2,019,324 bytes with 1,296 `data` entries and no continuation field. Every entry had `id` and `object: "model"`. None had `name`. `model_name` was a string on every entry and is not read. `context_length` was an integer on 1,276 entries and null on 20, so those 1,276 discovered models carry a context length. A saved credential sends `Authorization: Bearer` to the same URL. The authenticated 200 body was not observed. `GET /v3/models?view=models` is also public (526 grouped entries on this date) and is not the URL Arca derives.

The [Gemma 4 31B IT](https://www.edenai.co/models/gemma-4-31b-it) page and the live default catalog agree on the free endpoint id `google/gemma-4-31b-it`: `context_length` 262144, `owned_by` `google`, and `pricing.input_cost_per_token` / `output_cost_per_token` both `0.0`. The grouped view lists eight endpoints for the routable name `gemma-4-31b-it` and names `google/gemma-4-31b-it` as `pricing_endpoint`. The other seven have nonzero token prices: `arkanecloud/gemma4-31b`, `flexai/gemma-4-31b-it`, `flexai/gemma-4-31b-it@eu`, `greenference/gemma-4-31b-it`, `deepinfra/google/gemma-4-31B-it`, `infomaniak/google/gemma-4-31B-it`, and `greenpt/gemma4`. The bare name `gemma-4-31b-it` is not in the default list. The catalog has no free/paid flag, and discovered summaries keep `id`, `name`, and `context_length` only, so this price split is not encoded. The same anonymous list also had zero token rates on `google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it`, `together_ai/Prism-ML/Ternary-Bonsai-27B`, and four `cloudflare/@cf/...-lora` ids. [Plans & Pricing](https://www.edenai.co/docs/v3/overview/plans-prices) says self-serve access is pay as you go, that credits are purchased and consumed as calls are made, and that a 5.5% platform fee is applied at checkout. The catalog does not encode that credit balance.

Only `api.edenai.run` is mapped for host inference. `api.eu.edenai.run` is the documented EU host and answered `GET /v3/models` with a different 358-entry catalog that did not include `google/gemma-4-31b-it`, so it stays `custom`. `app.edenai.run` is the account app, `mcp.edenai.run` is the MCP host, and `edenai.co`, `www.edenai.co`, and `docs.edenai.co` are the marketing and docs sites. `edenai.run`, `www.edenai.run`, `www.api.edenai.run`, `api.edenai.co`, `docs.edenai.run`, and `app.edenai.co` did not resolve. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. The template portal is <https://app.edenai.run/settings/api-keys> with kind `api-key`. An unsigned GET of that path returns the app shell (title `Eden AI`).
