`nararouter` / `NaraRouter` is a gateway Provider for [NaraRouter](https://router.bynara.id/docs) by byNara (PT Nara Pemecut Digital). The [API docs](https://router.bynara.id/docs) and [llms.txt](https://router.bynara.id/llms.txt) set the OpenAI-compatible base URL to `https://router.bynara.id/v1`. They document `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/responses`, and `POST /v1/messages`. Examples and llms.txt send `Authorization: Bearer`. Keys use the prefix `sk-nry-`. llms.txt sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to `https://router.bynara.id` and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` to the same key. The docs say to point the Anthropic SDK at the base URL and send the key as a Bearer token. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://router.bynara.id/v1`, stores Responses on that same base URL, and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://router.bynara.id`. No Gemini format is documented.

Probes on 2026-11-08: `GET /v1/models`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/responses`, `POST /v1/messages`, and `GET /v1/nonexistent` with no key return HTTP 401 `unauthorized` (`A valid API key is required.`). A bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` on `GET /v1/models`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, and `POST /v1/messages` return that same 401 text, so this probe does not show whether the header was read as a credential. `POST /messages` without `/v1` returns the website 404, which matches the documented Anthropic base (the SDK appends `/v1/messages`). `POST /v1beta/models/deepseek-v4-flash:generateContent` and `GET /v1beta/models` return the website 404. `POST /v1/models/deepseek-v4-flash:generateContent` is under `/v1` and returns the same 401 as every other `/v1` path, including an unknown path, so it does not show a Gemini route. Gemini is not stored. `GET /openapi.json` is the website 404.

The docs call Responses stateful. They do not require `store: false`. `POST /v1/responses` with `store: true` and with `store: false` both return the same 401 before a body is checked, so no Responses storage override is declared. Embeddings (`POST /v1/embeddings`), rerank, System One, and the MCP route are on the same host. Image and video routes are on `https://api-images.bynara.id`. Those are not Arca chat protocols and are not stored.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` does not return a catalog. The docs say the authenticated list is exactly the aliases the caller's plan entitles, and that each model has a `reasoning` flag. The live authenticated body was not observed. There is no published OpenAPI document. When a credential is configured, model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder on `GET /v1/models`. That decoder reads `id`. The separate public catalog uses a UUID `id` plus `modelId` for the alias; that shape is not assumed for `/v1/models`.

With no credential, Arca does not call the network. The seed is the current [published catalog](https://router.bynara.id/api/public/model-catalog) (`object: "list"`, 36 `model_type: "chat"` and `status: "available"` on 2026-11-10, including `deepseek-v4.1-flash-abliterated`), in that array's order, with each `display_name` and `limits.contextWindow`. [llms.txt](https://router.bynara.id/llms.txt) points model aliases at [/ai-models](https://router.bynara.id/ai-models) and that JSON. The docs Models section is loaded live from [`GET /api/plans`](https://router.bynara.id/api/plans) and is not itself a model list in the static page. That plans payload adds `step-5-preview`, which is also an alias on [`GET /api/pricing`](https://router.bynara.id/api/pricing) (`display_name` `Step 5 Preview`, `max_context_tokens` 1000000) and is seeded. The docs quickstart and FAQ use the virtual id `auto/bynara`. It is not in the catalog or the pricing list, so it is seeded with no display name and no context. `kimi-k2.7-code` is seeded without a context: the catalog JSON says 262000 and the pricing API says 1000000. Pricing display names `DeepSeek v4 Flash 0731` and `DeepSeek v4 Pro 0813` are labels for the catalog aliases `deepseek-v4-flash` and `deepseek-v4-pro`; the seed uses the catalog names. Prose examples `kimi-k2.5` and `gemini-3.1-pro` are not in the catalog, plans, or pricing list and are not seeded. `:online` is a request suffix, not a separate id. `combo/free-pack` is a private user combo example. Pricing-only aliases that are not in the published directory or the plans list are not seeded, including `gpt-image-2`, `jev-1.14.0`, and `crouton-alpha`. `deepseek-v4.1-flash-abliterated` is in the 2026-11-10 chat catalog and is seeded. Three catalog rows (`claude-haiku-5.5`, `claude-opus-5.5`, `claude-sonnet-5.5`) set `responsesApi` to false and do not set `anthropicMessages`. The gateway docs still define those two endpoints, so both protocols stay registered.

The [pricing page](https://bynara.id/pricing) says the free plan is Rp 0 per day, 7M tokens per day, and 15 requests per minute, on Laguna S 2.1. `GET /api/plans` agrees: code `free`, `token_cap_daily` 7000000, `rpm_limit` 15, `price_daily_idr` 0, description "No card required.", models `["laguna-s-2.1"]`. The catalog does not encode that quota.

Only `router.bynara.id` is mapped for host inference. The site and the API share that host. `www.router.bynara.id` resolves to the same addresses, and a TLS handshake to it failed, so it stays `custom`. `bynara.id` is the marketing site and stays `custom`. `api.bynara.id`, `www.bynara.id`, and `docs.bynara.id` did not resolve. `router.bynara.web.id` appears in one docs combo example and returned Cloudflare HTTP 525. `api-images.bynara.id` is the image and video host. Those stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. `GET /keys` is HTTP 307 to `/login` (middleware rewrite `/en/keys`). `GET /register` is HTTP 200 (rewrite `/en/register`). The template portal is <https://router.bynara.id/register> with kind `console`.

## Zro — 2026-11-07

`zro` / `Zro` is a gateway Provider for [Zro](https://zro.moonmath.ai/docs/quickstart) by MoonMath.ai. The [quickstart](https://zro.moonmath.ai/docs/quickstart) and [API reference](https://zro.moonmath.ai/docs/api) set the OpenAI-compatible base URL to `https://zro.moonmath.ai/v1` and the Messages base URL to `https://zro.moonmath.ai`. They document `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/responses`, and `POST /v1/messages`. Examples send `Authorization: Bearer`. The Messages example sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to the host and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` to the same key. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://zro.moonmath.ai/v1`, stores Responses on that same base URL, and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://zro.moonmath.ai`. No Gemini format is documented.

Probes on 2026-11-07: `GET /v1/models`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/responses`, and `POST /v1/messages` with no key return HTTP 401 `auth_error` (`Authentication Error, No api key passed in.`). A bogus Bearer key on those routes returns HTTP 401 `token_not_found_in_db`. A bogus `x-api-key` on `GET /v1/models`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, and `POST /v1/messages` returns that same invalid-token 401, so Arca's Messages `x-api-key` header is accepted as a credential. `GET /v1/nonexistent` returns HTTP 404 `Not Found` with or without a bogus Bearer key, so an unknown path is not checked as a credential. `POST /messages` without `/v1` returns the website 404, which matches the documented Anthropic base (the SDK appends `/v1/messages`). `POST /v1beta/models/deepseek-v4.1-flash:generateContent` returns the website 404. `POST /v1/models/deepseek-v4.1-flash:generateContent` returns HTTP 405 `Method Not Allowed`. Gemini is not stored.

The published [OpenAPI](https://zro.moonmath.ai/openapi.json) lists `GET /models`, `POST /chat/completions`, and `POST /messages` on server `https://zro.moonmath.ai/v1`, with bearer auth. It describes `GET /models` as `{data: [{id, object, owned_by}]}` (`object` example `model`). It does not list `/responses`. The API reference does, and the live route is authenticated rather than unknown, so Responses is stored. The reference does not require `store: false` or reject `store: true`. Authentication fails before a request body is checked, so no Responses storage override is declared.

Anonymous `GET /v1/models` does not return a catalog. The live authenticated body was not observed. When a credential is configured, model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder on `GET /v1/models`, following that published schema. With no credential, Arca does not call the network and seeds the current ids from the [models](https://zro.moonmath.ai/docs/models) page: `deepseek-v4.1-flash` (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash), `glm-5.3` (GLM-5.3), `glm-5.3-flash` (GLM-5.3 Flash), `dolly1-security` (Dolly 1 Security), `dolly2-security` (Dolly 2 Security), `abyss1` (Abyss 1), and the virtual id `auto`. The six models are documented with a 1M context (1,048,576 tokens). `auto` has no context of its own. Documented `zro/` aliases resolve to the same models and are not seeded again. Retired ids (`glm-5.2`, `minimax-m3`, `kimi-k3`, `kimi-k2.7-code`, `deepseek-v4-flash-0731`, `laguna-2.1`, `deepseek-v4-flash-vision`, `minimax-m3.1-flash`) are not seeded. On this date, unsigned `GET /models` (a site rewrite, not `/v1/models`) and [llms.txt](https://zro.moonmath.ai/llms.txt) listed only `deepseek-v4.1-flash`, `glm-5.3`, and `glm-5.3-flash`. The models page and the [pricing](https://zro.moonmath.ai/pricing) page are the seed. Pricing says the free plan includes daily usage on GLM-5.3 Flash and DeepSeek V4.1 Flash and does not print a token count. The catalog does not encode that quota.

Only `zro.moonmath.ai` is mapped for host inference. The site and the API share that host. `www.zro.moonmath.ai` and `docs.zro.moonmath.ai` did not resolve. `moonmath.ai` redirects to the API host and stays `custom`, as do lookalikes. Keys are created after sign-in under Account → API keys. [Authentication](https://zro.moonmath.ai/auth.md) names `https://zro.moonmath.ai/api-keys`, and a signed-out GET of that path returns the site 404 (`Page not found`). The template portal is <https://zro.moonmath.ai/sign-in> with kind `console`.

## LLM7.io — 2026-11-07

`llm7` / `LLM7.io` is a gateway Provider for [LLM7.io](https://llm7.io). The [API introduction](https://docs.llm7.io/api-reference/introduction) sets the base URL to `https://api.llm7.io/v1` and says the API uses Bearer tokens. The [quickstart](https://docs.llm7.io/quickstart) configures the OpenAI client with that base URL and an API token from dash.llm7.io, then calls chat completions. [Function calling](https://docs.llm7.io/guides/function-calling) posts to `https://api.llm7.io/v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`. The [streaming guide](https://docs.llm7.io/guides/streaming) uses the same OpenAI client. Arca stores Chat Completions on `https://api.llm7.io/v1` (that base plus `/chat/completions`). The quickstart's selectors `default`, `fast`, and `pro` are chat model arguments. No model IDs are hard-coded.

The published [docs index](https://docs.llm7.io/llms.txt) and [OpenAPI document](https://docs.llm7.io/api-reference/openapi.json) cover the models list, image generation and edits, and asynchronous video tasks. They do not define an OpenAI Responses API, an Anthropic Messages API, or a Gemini chat contract. Image and video routes are outside Arca chat models, so they are not stored.

Probes on 2026-11-07: `POST /v1/chat/completions` with model `Inkling-Small` and no key returns HTTP 401 `missing_api_key` (`Missing API key.`). A bogus Bearer key on that model returns HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key` (`Your API key is invalid, expired, or revoked. Generate a new key at https://dash.llm7.io/#/api-keys`). The same route with turbo model `codestral-latest` returns HTTP 200 `chat.completion` with no key and with a bogus Bearer key. An empty body with no key returns HTTP 400 `invalid_json_request_body`; `{}` with a bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 400 `invalid_request`. `POST /v1/responses` with no key and with a bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 404 `upstream_not_found` (`Upstream provider endpoint was not found.`). `POST /v1/messages` with `Inkling-Small` returns HTTP 400 `unsupported_model_schema` with no key and with a bogus `x-api-key`. The same path with `claude-haiku-4-5` returns HTTP 401 `missing_api_key` with no key; a bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` both return HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key`. `POST /v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent` with no key returns HTTP 401 `missing_api_key`; a bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-goog-api-key` return HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key`. `POST /v1beta/nope` returns HTTP 404 `not_found`. `GET /v1/nonexistent` returns HTTP 404 `not_found` (`Not found.`) with or without a bogus Bearer key. `POST /v1/images/generations` with no key returns HTTP 401 `missing_api_key`. `GET /` and `GET /v1` on the API host return that same 404. Responses, Anthropic Messages, and Gemini generateContent are not stored: the public docs do not define those chat contracts.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential. [List models](https://docs.llm7.io/api-reference/endpoint/models-list) documents `GET /v1/models` as `{object: "list", data: [{id, object: "model", ...}]}` and sets `security: []`. The [Models API guide](https://docs.llm7.io/guides/models-api) shows the same anonymous curl. Anonymous `GET /v1/models` on this date returned HTTP 200 with that shape and 70 `data` entries. A bogus Bearer key also returned HTTP 200 with 70 entries. The decoder reads `id` and `name`. Every entry had `id` and `object: "model"`. None had `name`. Every `created` was an integer, and every `owned_by` was an empty string. `context_window` was an object on every entry (`tokens` was an integer on 41 and null on 29), so discovered models do not carry a context length from this list. The response had no continuation token. No models are hard-coded. On this date `model_type` was chat 51, image 8, video 6, audio_to_text 4, and systemone 1. `schema_endpoints` was `openai` on 47, `anthropic` and `openai` on 15, `gemini` and `openai` on 3, `audio` on 4, and `systemone` on 1. `tier` was `pro` on 66 and `turbo` on 4: `codestral-latest`, `gpt-oss:20b`, `mistral-Nemo-Instruct-2407`, and `nemotron-3-nano:30b`. The three `gemini` records were the image models `gemini-3.0-pro-image`, `gemini-3.1-flash-image`, and `gemini-nano-banana-2.1`; each `api_interfaces` entry listed `POST /v1/images/generations` and Gemini `generateContent` paths. Confirmed ids include `Inkling-Small` (chat, pro, `schema_endpoints` `openai`), `claude-haiku-4-5` (chat, pro, `anthropic` and `openai`), `codestral-latest`, the docs example `gpt-image-2`, and the docs example `seedance-2.0-mini`. The documented selectors `default`, `fast`, and `pro`, and the models-guide example `gpt-5.4`, were not in the list.

Only `api.llm7.io` is mapped for host inference. `GET https://llm7.io/v1/models` returns the website HTML (HTTP 404). `www.llm7.io` redirects to `https://llm7.io/`. `dash.llm7.io` serves the dashboard shell (title `Token LLM7.io`, canonical `https://dash.llm7.io/`), and `docs.llm7.io` serves the documentation site, so those hosts stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. The invalid-key error publishes <https://dash.llm7.io/#/api-keys>. The dashboard bundle accepts that hash route for API keys. The template portal is that URL with kind `api-key`.

The [limits](https://docs.llm7.io/limits) page says free-token text access is 100,000 tokens per 24 hours, with 1 request per second, 60 per minute, and 250 per hour. The Models API page states the same token amount per day and the same request windows, and both pages say free-token quotas may be reduced without notice. The catalog does not encode that quota.
## EdgeOne Makers Models — 2026-11-07

`edgeone-makers` / `EdgeOne Makers Models` is a gateway Provider for Tencent EdgeOne Makers' unified model gateway. The [integration guide](https://pages.edgeone.ai/document/makers-models-integration) (Chinese edition: [Makers Models 集成](https://makers.edgeone.link/document/makers-models-integration)) sets the OpenAI client base URL to `https://ai-gateway.edgeone.link/v1` and calls `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`. The [Anthropic vendor page](https://pages.edgeone.ai/document/models-vendor-keys-anthropic) sets the Anthropic SDK base URL to `https://ai-gateway.edgeone.link` and calls `POST /v1/messages` with `X-Api-Key`. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://ai-gateway.edgeone.link/v1` and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://ai-gateway.edgeone.link`. Built-in model IDs use the `@makers/` prefix. Self-funded vendor models use `<vendor>/<model>` on the same gateway key and base URL; those IDs depend on a vendor key bound in the console and are not part of the seed.

The same integration guide and the [models overview](https://pages.edgeone.ai/document/models) say the gateway is compatible with the OpenAI SDK, the Anthropic SDK, and the Vercel AI SDK. They do not document an OpenAI Responses API or a Gemini generateContent route. Probes on 2026-11-07: `POST /v1/chat/completions` with no key returns HTTP 401 `auth_missing` (`Missing API key`); a bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 401 `auth_failed` (`Invalid API key`). `POST /v1/messages` with no key returns the same 401 `auth_missing`. A bogus `X-Api-Key` and a bogus Bearer key, each sent with `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, return HTTP 401 `auth_failed`. `POST /messages` without `/v1` returns HTTP 404 `Invalid request path.`, which matches the documented Anthropic base (the SDK appends `/v1/messages`). `POST /v1/responses` with no key or a bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 401 from the models service (`x-makers-models-id` is present), but the public docs do not describe a Responses contract, so Responses is not stored. `POST /v1beta/models/@makers/deepseek-v4-flash:generateContent` with `x-goog-api-key` returns HTTP 404 `Invalid request path.`, so Gemini is not stored. `POST /v1/nonexistent` returns that same edge 404.

`GET`, `HEAD`, `OPTIONS`, and `POST` on `/v1/models` return HTTP 404 `Invalid request path.` with or without a bogus Bearer key. Those responses have no `x-makers-models-id`, so the path is rejected before the models service. No reviewed HTTP discovery spec is registered, and the generic OpenAI `/v1/models` fallback is not used. The catalog seed is only the built-in IDs printed on the [models overview](https://pages.edgeone.ai/document/models-vendors-overview) (the [Chinese overview](https://makers.edgeone.link/document/models-vendors-overview) lists the same IDs): `@makers/hy3`, `@makers/hy3-preview`, `@makers/deepseek-v4-pro`, `@makers/deepseek-v4-flash`, `@makers/deepseek-v4.1-flash`, `@makers/minimax-m3`, `@makers/minimax-m2.7`, `@makers/kimi-k2.6`, and `@makers/zhuque-text`. The page does not give separate display names or context lengths, so the seed stores neither. The overview says those IDs must match exactly and that the list may change; copy a current ID from the console or that page. Vendor examples such as `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5` are not seeded. An explicit Models list URL on a connection still uses the OpenAI catalog decoder.

The [limits](https://pages.edgeone.ai/document/limits-and-quotas) page lists the free-edition built-in model quota as 500,000 tokens/month and says the table may be adjusted after commercialization. The [Chinese free LLM page](https://pages.edgeone.ai/zh/use-cases/free-llm-api) says new accounts receive 500,000 free tokens as a limited-time grant, and that the free quota is shared across every project on the same account. The overview says built-in models are for technical validation and prototyping and should not be used in production. The catalog does not encode the quota.

Only `ai-gateway.edgeone.link` is mapped for host inference. `makers.edgeone.link`, `pages.edgeone.ai`, `edgeone.ai`, `console.tencentcloud.com`, and lookalikes stay `custom`. Keys are created on the Makers / Models / API Key page after sign-in. The [content creator template](https://pages.edgeone.ai/templates/content-creator-agent) links the console through `https://edgeone.ai/makers/new?s_url=https://console.tencentcloud.com/edgeone/makers`. An unsigned request to `https://console.tencentcloud.com/edgeone/makers` redirects to the Tencent Cloud login page with that console path as `s_url`. No stable direct key-list URL is published, so the template portal is <https://console.tencentcloud.com/edgeone/makers> with kind `console`.

## ASI:Cloud — 2026-10-29

`asi-cloud` / `ASI:Cloud` is a gateway Provider for CUDOS / Singularity Compute's general inference service. The official [inference quickstart](https://docs.cudos.org/docs/asi-cloud/inference/quickstart) and [Chat Completions guide](https://docs.cudos.org/docs/asi-cloud/inference/tutorials/chat-completions) configure the OpenAI client with `https://inference.asicloud.cudos.org/v1`, use Bearer API-key authentication, and send ordinary conversational messages. Arca stores Chat Completions on that base URL with `/chat/completions`. The guides use model IDs such as `asi1-mini`; model IDs and promotional quotas are not hard-coded in the Provider template. Responses, Anthropic Messages and Gemini formats are not registered without their own reviewed service contract.

The [Super Decider introduction](https://docs.cudos.org/docs/asi-cloud/inference/super-decider/introduction) and [API contract](https://github.com/Singularity-Compute/super-decider/blob/main/docs/API.md) describe a separate decision service at `https://llm.a.singularitycompute.com/v1`. It expects a JSON question schema and JSON state in exactly two messages, and returns structured answers rather than general conversational prose. That host is not the ASI:Cloud chat template and remains `custom`.

The successful authenticated `GET /v1/models` JSON body on the general inference service has not been verified. No reviewed discovery spec or fixed model list is registered; discovery retains the existing OpenAI `/v1/models` fallback. This does not claim the private catalog shape was observed. Only the exact documented host `inference.asicloud.cudos.org` is mapped to `asi-cloud`; the console, marketing sites, Super Decider endpoint and lookalikes remain `custom`. The portal is the [ASI:Cloud console](https://asicloud.cudos.org/) with kind `console`, because a stable direct key-list URL has not been verified.

## Helmholtz Blablador — 2026-10-27

`helmholtz-blablador` / `Helmholtz Blablador` is a gateway Provider for [Blablador](http://blablador.fz-juelich.de) (AI@JSC). The [API access](https://sdlaml.pages.jsc.fz-juelich.de/ai/guides/blablador_api_access/) guide says Blablador lets researchers add models to an authenticated web interface and also offers a REST API. That page sets the endpoint to `https://api.blablador.fz-juelich.de/v1/` and says the endpoints are compatible with the OpenAI Python API. It says each model provides endpoints according to its own capabilities, and names chat completions, text completions, and embeddings. Arca uses Chat Completions on `https://api.blablador.fz-juelich.de/v1` (that base plus `/chat/completions`). The guide does not print the `POST /v1/chat/completions` path itself. Text completions and embeddings are outside Arca chat models. The guide does not document an OpenAI Responses API, Anthropic Messages, or a Gemini format, so none is stored.

The guide's curl example sends `Authorization: Bearer` to `GET https://api.blablador.fz-juelich.de/v1/models` and prints an OpenAI list (`object: "list"`, `data: [{id, object: "model", owned_by, ...}]`). The printed ids are aliases such as `alias-code` and `alias-fast`. Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential. No models are hard-coded. The guide says aliases let the backing model change without breaking callers, that those aliases are not shown on the web interface, and that experimental aliases come and go. It points at `GET /v1/models` for the current list. The pasted example includes a trailing comma before the closing `]`, so that sample is not strict JSON as printed. The live list body was not observed, because the route rejects a missing key. The generic OpenAI decoder reads `id` and does not map the example's `max_model_len` field.

Keys are Helmholtz Codebase GitLab personal access tokens. The guide says to register at [Helmholtz Codebase](https://codebase.helmholtz.cloud) with any EduGAIN account, then create a token on [Access Tokens](https://codebase.helmholtz.cloud/-/user_settings/personal_access_tokens) with the `read_user` scope. Tokens are valid for at most one year. The template portal is that Access Tokens page with kind `api-key`. An unsigned request to that page redirects to `https://codebase.helmholtz.cloud/users/sign_in`.

Probes on 2026-10-27: `GET /v1/models` and `POST /v1/chat/completions` with no key, and the same requests with a bogus Bearer key, return HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key` (`You must provide a valid API key.`). `POST /v1/completions` and `POST /v1/embeddings` with no key return that same 401. `POST /v1/responses`, `POST /v1/messages`, `GET /v1/nonexistent`, `GET /models`, `GET /v1`, and `GET /` also return that 401, including a bogus Bearer key on `GET /v1/nonexistent`. A presented token is checked before routing, so the status does not prove which of those paths exist. `https://blablador.fz-juelich.de/v1/models` and `http://blablador.fz-juelich.de/v1/models` return HTTP 200 HTML for the web app.

Only `api.blablador.fz-juelich.de` is mapped for host inference. `blablador.fz-juelich.de` stays `custom`. `www.blablador.fz-juelich.de` did not resolve on this date and stays `custom`, as do lookalikes. The guide's `rel=canonical` is <https://sdlaml.gitlab.io/guides/blablador_api_access/>. Arca's docs link is the JSC pages URL above. The guide documents per-category request windows, HTTP 429 for a throttled request, and HTTP 403 for a banned user. The catalog does not encode those limits. The guide does not state a price.

## decuff — 2026-10-27

`decuff` / `decuff` is a gateway Provider for [decuff](https://decuff.ai/docs). That page sets the OpenAI base URL to `https://api.decuff.ai/v1` and the Anthropic base URL to `https://api.decuff.ai`. It documents `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/responses` (stateless; send `store: false`), and `POST /v1/messages`. OpenAI examples send `Authorization: Bearer`. The Claude Code example sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to the host and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` to the same `dc_sk_` key. The Codex example uses the OpenAI base with `wire_api = "responses"`. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://api.decuff.ai/v1`, stores Responses on that same base URL, and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://api.decuff.ai`. The page also documents `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens`. That is not an Arca chat protocol, so it is not stored. No Gemini format is documented.

Probes on 2026-10-27: `GET /v1/models`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/responses`, and `POST /v1/messages` with no key return HTTP 401 `authentication_error` (`Missing API key. Send it as Authorization: Bearer <key>, or x-api-key for Anthropic clients`). A bogus Bearer key on those routes, and a bogus `x-api-key` on Messages, return HTTP 401 `Invalid or revoked API key`. `GET /v1/nonexistent` returns HTTP 404 `not_found_error` (`Unknown endpoint. OpenAI clients need a base URL ending in /v1; Anthropic clients use the host alone`) with or without a bogus key, so an unknown path is not checked as a credential. `POST /messages` without `/v1` returns that 404, which matches the documented Anthropic base (the SDK appends `/v1/messages`). `POST /v1beta/models/decuffed-flash:generateContent` returns the same 404, so Gemini is not stored.

`GET /v1/models` is documented as the list of models the key can call. The gateway source linked from that page returns an OpenAI list (`object: "list"`, `data: [{id, object: "model", owned_by}]`) plus a Codex `models` array, and the same page says published source does not prove what is running. The live list body was not observed, because the route is 401 without a key. No reviewed discovery spec is registered. No models are hard-coded. The docs example model is `decuffed-flash`, and the docs say the names on the [models page](https://decuff.ai/models) work too.

Public `GET /v1/catalog`, named by `GET /v1/service` as `catalogURL`, returns HTTP 200 with or without a bogus Bearer key. On 2026-10-27 the body was `{object: "list", checkedAt, models: [...]}` and included `decuffed-flash` (`name` `decuffed-qwen3.8-27b`, Qwen3.8, 27B, context 65536, upstream `munekazu/Huihui-Qwen3.8-27B-abliterated-FP8`) and `decuffed-flagship` (`name` `decuffed-deepseek-v4.1-flash`, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, 552B / 16B active, context 262144). That document uses `models` rather than the OpenAI `data` array, so it is not the discovery route. The models page describes abliterated weights as weights edited to remove learned refusals.

`GET /v1/service` on the same date lists `protocols` as `chat_completions`, `responses_stateless`, `messages`, and `count_tokens`, and `name` as `Decuffed`. The site brand and this template stay `decuff`. Its `allowances` set `freeMonthlyTokens` to 1000000 for verification methods `phone` and `google`, on tiers Flash, Standard, and Flagship, and `freeUnverifiedTokens` to 500000 on Flash only. The docs do not print those amounts. They point at `allowances.freeUnverifiedTokens` (0 means none) and say a verified phone adds the free allowance. The catalog does not encode a quota.

Only `api.decuff.ai` is mapped for host inference. `decuff.ai` serves the website (`GET /v1/models` there returns the site HTML), and `www.decuff.ai` redirects to that site, so both stay `custom`. `api.decuffed.com` returns the same 401 and 404 as the documented API, and the site client allows that origin, but the docs publish only `api.decuff.ai`, so it stays `custom`, as do lookalikes. The docs also show a Tor onion for the same routes; Arca stores the clearnet bases only. Keys are created after sign-in. The docs do not publish a stable direct key-list URL, so the template portal is <https://decuff.ai/account> with kind `console`.

## Token360 — 2026-10-27

`token360` / `Token360` is a gateway Provider for [Token360](https://www.token360.ai/en-US/docs/api-reference/overview). That page sets the OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic Messages, and platform base URL to `https://api.token360.ai/v1` and the Google Gemini native base URL to `https://api.token360.ai/v1beta`. It documents four LLM request styles: `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`, `POST /v1/responses` (stateless; the page says Token360 rejects `previous_response_id`, `conversation`, `background: true`, and explicit `store: true`), `POST /v1/messages` with `Authorization: Bearer` or `x-api-key`, and `POST /v1beta/models/{model}:generateContent` plus `:streamGenerateContent` with `x-goog-api-key` or the Google `key` query parameter. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default on `https://api.token360.ai/v1`, stores Responses on that same base URL, stores Anthropic Messages at `https://api.token360.ai`, and stores Gemini generateContent at `https://api.token360.ai/v1beta`. The same page says `countTokens` is not supported. `POST /v1/videos`, image generation, audio, async chat, and batch jobs are outside Arca chat models, so they are not stored. No Gemini Interactions format is documented.

Probes on 2026-10-27: `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `POST /v1/responses` with no key return HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key` (`Missing or invalid Authorization header`); a bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 401 `API Key Invalid`. `POST /v1/messages` with no key returns HTTP 401 in the Anthropic error envelope (`authentication_error`); a bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-api-key` return HTTP 401 `API Key Invalid`. `POST /v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent` with no key returns HTTP 401 Google `UNAUTHENTICATED` (`Missing or invalid Authorization header`); a bogus Bearer key and a bogus `x-goog-api-key` return HTTP 401 `API Key Invalid`. `GET /v1/nonexistent` returns HTTP 404 `not_found_error` with or without a bogus Bearer key. `POST /messages` without `/v1` returns HTTP 404, which matches the documented Anthropic base (the SDK appends `/v1/messages`). `POST /v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:countTokens` returns HTTP 501 `countTokens is not supported by Token360 in this release`. `POST /v1beta/interactions` returns HTTP 404, so Gemini Interactions is not stored. `POST /v1/videos` and `POST /v1/images/generations` return HTTP 401, but those modalities are not stored. The Google `key` query parameter and an explicit `store: true` rejection were not probed; authentication fails before either can be observed.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential. [List models](https://www.token360.ai/en-US/docs/api-reference/models/list-models) documents `GET /v1/models` as `{object: "list", data: [{id, object: "model", owned_by, ...}]}` and says no authentication is required. Anonymous `GET /v1/models` on this date returned HTTP 200 with that shape and 123 `data` entries. A bogus Bearer key also returned HTTP 200. The decoder reads `id` and `name`. The documented example `created` is an integer; every live `created` value in that response was a decimal string. `context_length` was null on every entry, so discovered models do not carry a context length from this list. The same route accepts `current` and `size`. `size=1` returned one entry and `current=2` returned the next, while the request with no query parameters returned the same 123 entries as `size=1000`. Arca does not send those page parameters. No models are hard-coded. Confirmed ids in that list include `minimax-m3.1-flash` (name `MiniMax M3.1 Flash`, owned_by `MiniMax`), the docs example `claude-opus-5`, and the list-models example `qwen3.5-flash`. `GET /public/models` is a different document (`data.list[].name`, with its own pagination) and is not the discovery route. The catalog does not encode a promotional free window.

Only `api.token360.ai` is mapped for host inference. `www.token360.ai` serves the website (`GET /v1/models` redirects to `/en-US/v1/models`), and `token360.ai` returns HTTP 404, so both stay `custom`, as do lookalikes. `admin.token360.ai` appears as an access-control allow origin on some API 404s and serves an HTML shell, but the docs do not publish it as an inference host, so it stays `custom`. Keys are created in the console after sign-in. The docs do not publish a stable direct key-list URL (`/en-US/console` and `/en-US/api-keys` return HTTP 404), so the template portal is <https://www.token360.ai/en-US> with kind `console`.

## Pojia AI — 2026-10-26

`pojiaai` / `Pojia AI` is a gateway Provider for [Pojia AI](https://pojia.ai/) (破甲). The site configuration published on that page sets `api_base_url` to `https://api.pojia.ai` and lists the same host as the general endpoint. Probes on 2026-10-26: `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `GET /v1/models` with no key return HTTP 401 `API_KEY_REQUIRED` (`API key is required in Authorization header (Bearer scheme), x-api-key header, or x-goog-api-key header`); a bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 401 `INVALID_API_KEY`. `GET /v1/nonexistent` returns HTTP 404 `404 page not found`, so those two routes are authenticated rather than unknown. Arca uses Chat Completions on `https://api.pojia.ai/v1`. `POST /v1/responses` and `POST /v1/messages` also return the invalid-key error, but the public site does not document those protocols, so neither is stored. The models route is authenticated and its JSON shape is not documented, so no reviewed discovery spec is registered. No models are hard-coded. The catalog does not encode relay pricing or promotional codes.

Only `api.pojia.ai` is mapped for host inference. `pojia.ai` (the site and [Keys](https://pojia.ai/keys) page) and `pojia.org` also answer `/v1/models` with the same 401, but the published base host is `api.pojia.ai`, so the apex, `pojia.org`, and lookalikes resolve to `custom`. The template portal is <https://pojia.ai/keys> with kind `api-key`.

## Mango Inference — 2026-10-26

`mango-inference` / `Mango Inference` is a gateway Provider for [Mango Inference](https://inference.mangoboost.io/docs/concepts/openai-compatibility/). That page sets the OpenAI client base URL to `https://api.mangoboost.io/v1` and documents `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`. The [Messages](https://inference.mangoboost.io/docs/reference/messages/) reference documents `POST https://api.mangoboost.io/v1/messages` with the same key in `x-api-key` (Anthropic SDKs also send `anthropic-version`). Arca uses Chat Completions on `https://api.mangoboost.io/v1` and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://api.mangoboost.io`. The compatibility page says `POST /v1/responses` is not supported. Probes on 2026-10-26: `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `GET /v1/models`, and `POST /v1/messages` with no key or a bogus Bearer key return HTTP 401 `invalid API key` (Messages uses the Anthropic error envelope). `POST /v1/responses` returns HTTP 404 `unknown endpoint`. `GET /v1/nonexistent` returns HTTP 404 even when a bogus Bearer key is presented, so a token is not required to tell an unknown path from a routed one.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential. [Models and pricing](https://inference.mangoboost.io/docs/reference/models-and-pricing/) documents `GET https://api.mangoboost.io/v1/models` as the OpenAI-compatible list, keyed by `id`, scoped to the key. No models are hard-coded. Only `api.mangoboost.io` is mapped for host inference. The console at <https://inference.mangoboost.io/> is the template portal with kind `console`; the docs do not publish a stable direct key-list URL. The catalog does not encode signup credit.

## Darkbloom — 2026-10-26

`darkbloom` / `Darkbloom` is a gateway Provider for the [Darkbloom API](https://docs.darkbloom.dev/quickstart-consumer). The quickstart and the [chat completions](https://docs.darkbloom.dev/reference/chat-completions) reference set the OpenAI base URL to `https://api.darkbloom.dev/v1`, call `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`, and say `POST /v1/responses` is an alias on the same handler. Arca uses Chat Completions as the default and stores Responses on that same base URL. No Anthropic Messages or Gemini format is documented. Probes on 2026-10-26: `GET /v1/models`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, and `POST /v1/responses` with no key return HTTP 401 `missing credentials — use Authorization: Bearer <token>`; a bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 401 `invalid API key`. `GET /v1/nonexistent` returns HTTP 404 `endpoint GET /v1/nonexistent is not implemented` with or without a bogus key. `POST /v1/messages` also returns the invalid-key error, but Messages is absent from the chat reference, so it is not stored.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential. The console and quickstart tell callers to read IDs from `GET /v1/models` / `client.models.list()`. No models are hard-coded. Only `api.darkbloom.dev` is mapped for host inference. `console.darkbloom.dev` and `docs.darkbloom.dev` resolve to `custom`. Keys are created in the API console at <https://console.darkbloom.dev/api-console>, which is the template portal with kind `api-key`. The quickstart describes the service as an experimental research prototype; the catalog does not encode an offer.

## ScaleDown — 2026-10-26

That page sets the OpenAI base URL to `https://api.scaledown.xyz/v1` (it explicitly says `api.scaledown.ai` is not an API host) and documents `POST /v1/chat/completions` for the extract and summarize models, with `GET /v1/models` returning an OpenAI list. The same page shows `x-api-key`. Probes on 2026-10-26: a missing key on `GET /v1/models` and `POST /v1/chat/completions` returns HTTP 401 `x-api-key header is required`. The same chat request with `Authorization: Bearer` or `x-api-key` set to an unfunded value returns HTTP 402 `Insufficient credits`, so Arca's Bearer header is accepted. `GET /v1/models` with either header returns HTTP 200 `{object: "list", data: [{id, ...}]}`. `POST /v1/responses`, `POST /v1/messages`, and `GET /v1/nonexistent` return HTTP 403 `Missing Authentication Token`, which is the gateway's unknown-route response, so no Responses or Messages endpoint is stored. Classify is documented on a separate Decisions path, not chat completions, and is not stored.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential, because the list is rejected without a key. No models are hard-coded. Only `api.scaledown.xyz` is mapped for host inference. `scaledown.ai`, `api.scaledown.ai`, and lookalikes resolve to `custom`. The product site does not publish a separate key-list URL, so the template portal is <https://scaledown.ai/> with kind `console`. The catalog does not encode a token grant.

Use /v1/models or /v1/chat/completions.` `GET /v1/models` and `POST /v1/chat/completions` return HTTP 503 with the maintenance message, including when `Authorization: Bearer free` is sent, so an auth check cannot be separated from the outage. `POST /v1/responses` and `POST /v1/messages` return that same 503, but the 404 names only models and chat completions, so no extra protocol is stored. Arca uses Chat Completions on `https://free.empero.org/v1`. No reviewed discovery spec is registered, because the list body was not observable. No model is hard-coded. The catalog does not encode a quota.

## LarpRouter — 2026-10-24

The live template moved to Lutheos on 2026-11-10. See Template corrections above. The notes below record the 2026-10-24 LarpRouter host.

`larprouter` / `LarpRouter` is a gateway Provider for [LarpRouter](https://larprouter.com), an OpenAI- and Anthropic-compatible model router. The [API reference](https://larprouter.com/docs) gives the OpenAI SDK Base URL `https://larprouter.com/v1` and calls `POST https://larprouter.com/v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer lrsk-...`. The same page sets the Anthropic SDK `base_url` to `https://larprouter.com` (the SDK appends `/v1/messages`) and calls `POST https://larprouter.com/v1/messages` with `x-api-key` or `Authorization: Bearer`. Arca therefore uses Chat Completions as the default protocol on `https://larprouter.com/v1` and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://larprouter.com`. No Gemini format is documented. The reference does not document an OpenAI Responses API, so none is stored.

The reference also documents `POST /v1/images/generations` and `POST /v1/systemone` (decision models). Those are outside Arca chat models. Its endpoint table includes keyless Posty chat at `POST /chat`. Probes on 2026-10-24: `GET` and `POST /v1/chat`, and `POST /chat`, return HTTP 404, so that path is not stored.

Probes without a key: `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `GET /v1/models` return HTTP 401 `missing_api_key` (`No LarpRouter API key provided.`). `POST /v1/messages` returns HTTP 401 in the Anthropic error envelope with the same message. A bogus `Authorization: Bearer` or `x-api-key` on those three paths returns HTTP 401 `Invalid API key.` (`invalid_api_key` on the OpenAI-shaped paths). `POST /v1/v1/messages` and `GET /v1/nonexistent` return HTTP 404 `unknown_endpoint`. That 404 lists the supported routes as `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/messages`, `/v1/images/generations`, and `/v1/models`. `GET /v1/responses` returns HTTP 400 `invalid_body` and `POST /v1/responses` returns HTTP 401, but Responses is absent from both the reference and that supported-route list, so it is not stored.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential. The reference lists `GET /models` as the models available to the key and says to use an exact returned model ID; the quickstart placeholders are `MODEL_ID_FROM_GET_API_V1_MODELS` and `MODEL_ID_FROM_CLIENT_MODELS_LIST`. The reference does not print the JSON body. `GET /v1/models` is routed and authenticated (401, unlike the 404 of unknown paths). No models are hard-coded. On this date the [catalog](https://larprouter.com/models) listed nine routes. `deepseek-v4.1-flash` and `glm-5.3-flash` are marked Free tier. The Free tier tooltip says the daily free allowance covers those models and that, once the day's allowance is used, they bill account credit at their published rate. The catalog does not encode offers.

Only `larprouter.com` is mapped for host inference. `www.larprouter.com` also answers `GET /v1/models` with HTTP 401, but the docs use the apex host, so `www` and lookalike hosts resolve to `custom`. Keys prefixed `lrsk-` are created from the dashboard. The public [Key management](https://larprouter.com/keys) page links to [`/api-keys`](https://larprouter.com/api-keys), which redirects unsigned requests to [`/sign-in`](https://larprouter.com/sign-in), so the template portal is <https://larprouter.com/api-keys> with kind `api-key`.

## BlazeRail — 2026-10-24

`blazerail` / `BlazeRail` is a gateway Provider for [BlazeRail](https://www.blazerail.com), an OpenAI-compatible gateway for frontier models. The [docs](https://www.blazerail.com/docs), [llms.txt](https://www.blazerail.com/llms.txt), and [OpenAPI](https://www.blazerail.com/openapi.json) set the OpenAI client base URL to `https://api.blazerail.com/v1` and call `POST https://api.blazerail.com/v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`. The OpenAPI production server is `https://api.blazerail.com`. Keys are created at [Dashboard → API Keys](https://www.blazerail.com/dashboard/api-keys). The documented example model is `openai/gpt-5.6-luna`. Arca therefore uses Chat Completions as the default protocol on `https://api.blazerail.com/v1`.

The OpenAPI document lists chat completions, `GET /v1/models`, and media, extraction, and memory routes. It does not list `/v1/responses`, `/v1/messages`, or a Gemini generateContent path. Probes on 2026-10-24: `POST /v1/chat/completions` with no key returns HTTP 401 `Authorization header required`; a bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 401 `Invalid authentication credentials.` `GET /v1`, `GET /`, `GET /v1/nonexistent`, `POST /v1/responses`, `POST /v1/messages`, `POST /v1/embeddings`, and `POST /anthropic/v1/messages` all return HTTP 502 `upstream unavailable`, so that status does not establish a route. No Responses, Anthropic Messages, or Gemini endpoint is stored. Image, video, audio, and extraction routes are out of scope for chat models.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential. The [models page](https://www.blazerail.com/models) and OpenAPI document `GET https://api.blazerail.com/v1/models`, and the models operation has no security requirement. A probe without a key returns HTTP 200 `{object: "list", data: [{id, object: "model", owned_by, context_length, ...}]}` (310 entries on this date). [llms.txt](https://www.blazerail.com/llms.txt) also names `GET /v1/models/{id}`, but that path is absent from the OpenAPI path list and a probe of an encoded id returns the same HTTP 502 as unknown routes, so discovery uses the list. No models are hard-coded.

The [free models page](https://www.blazerail.com/free) says a one-time $1 deposit unlocks `:free` model ids at $0 per token, with rate limits, and that the unsuffixed id is billed. Its example is `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash:free`. The OpenAPI chat description instead says `:free` routes require a one-time $10 deposit and answer HTTP 429 `free_tier_exhausted` when that allowance is spent. The catalogue does not encode either offer. On this date the public list included 30 ids ending in `:free` with zero prompt and completion prices, including that example. Ordinary ids on the same list carry a non-zero price. Both kinds use `https://api.blazerail.com/v1`.

Only `api.blazerail.com` is mapped for host inference. `www.blazerail.com` serves the site and `blazerail.com` redirects there; `GET /v1/models` on the site host is HTTP 404, so both resolve to `custom`, as do lookalike hosts.

## Stark Relay — 2026-10-24

`stark-relay` / `Stark Relay` is a gateway Provider for [Stark Relay](https://starkrelay.bond/), a Starknet-native relay for Claude, GPT, Grok, Gemini, GLM, DeepSeek, and other models behind one relay key. The [Start using your API key](https://app.notion.com/p/05-Start-using-your-API-key-3941472684838133afbfdbb70a0d82f4) page of the [User Guide](https://app.notion.com/p/Stark-Relay-User-Guide-3941472684838176864cd6c9135893a3) gives `https://starkrelay.bond/v1` for OpenAI-compatible clients, Codex CLI, and Opencode, and `https://starkrelay.bond` for Claude Code. Keys are created on [API Keys](https://starkrelay.bond/api-keys) after a Starknet-wallet signup ([Create your first API key](https://app.notion.com/p/04-Create-your-first-API-key-39414726848381b8b05df2bfd91349da)). The signed-in overview is the [Dashboard](https://starkrelay.bond/dashboard). Arca uses Chat Completions as the default protocol on `https://starkrelay.bond/v1`.

The [Codex](https://app.notion.com/p/07-Use-with-Codex-394147268483819daf55f922e37679b3) page configures Codex as an OpenAI-compatible Responses provider with `base_url = "https://starkrelay.bond/v1"` and `wire_api = "responses"`, authenticated as `OPENAI_API_KEY`. Responses is stored on that same Base URL. The [Claude Code](https://app.notion.com/p/06-Use-with-Claude-Code-3941472684838169a155e0911805c4ad) page sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=https://starkrelay.bond` and sends the relay key as `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`. Probes on 2026-10-24: `POST /v1/messages` with no key, a bogus `Authorization: Bearer`, or a bogus `x-api-key` returns HTTP 401 in the Anthropic error envelope (`Invalid API key`). `POST /v1/v1/messages` returns HTTP 404, so the live Messages path is `/v1/messages` on the bare host. Arca stores Anthropic Messages at `https://starkrelay.bond`, which is the base an Anthropic client uses when it appends `/v1/messages`. No Gemini format is documented. `POST /v1beta/models/gemini-3.6-flash:generateContent` is answered by the site shell (`Only HTML requests are supported here`), not an API route, so no Gemini endpoint is stored.

Probes without a key: `GET /v1/models`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, and `POST /v1/responses` return HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key` in the OpenAI error envelope. A bogus Bearer token returns the same 401 on those paths. `POST /v1/completions` and `POST /v1/nonexistent` return HTTP 404 `404 page not found`, including when a bogus Bearer token is presented, so a token is checked only after routing. `POST /v1/embeddings` is routed (401) and out of scope for chat models. The public guide does not describe the `GET /v1/models` list shape. No reviewed discovery spec is registered. Model IDs are copied from the Start dialog and are not hard-coded. The Codex example uses `gpt-5.5`. The homepage describes new-user credit and recharge campaigns; the catalog does not encode offers.

Only `starkrelay.bond` is mapped for host inference. `www.starkrelay.bond` redirects to the apex host and resolves to `custom`, as do lookalike hosts. The template portal is the API Keys page at <https://starkrelay.bond/api-keys> with kind `api-key`.

## InferX — 2026-10-24

`inferx` / `InferX` is a gateway Provider for [InferX Public Endpoints](https://inferx.net), the hosted OpenAI-compatible API. The [Quickstart](https://inferx.net/docs/quickstart), [SDK guides](https://inferx.net/docs/sdk-guides), and homepage configure the OpenAI client with `base_url="https://model.inferx.net/v1"` and `client.chat.completions.create`. [Authentication](https://inferx.net/docs/authentication) sends `Authorization: Bearer YOUR_API_KEY`. The [API reference](https://inferx.net/docs/api-reference) confirms that public inference calls use that chat-completions shape and says unconfirmed management routes stay unpublished. Arca therefore uses Chat Completions as the default protocol on `https://model.inferx.net/v1`.

Published endpoint pages, such as the [catalog](https://model.inferx.net/catalog/endpoints), also document a shared token-based base URL `https://model.inferx.net/endpoints/v1` for `POST /endpoints/v1/chat/completions`. Dedicated GPU serving uses a tenant-scoped URL under `https://model.inferx.net/funccall/<tenant>/endpoints/<model>/v1/chat/completions`, which the console supplies per account. Those paths share the host `model.inferx.net`, so host inference resolves them to this template. The saved default stays the quickstart base `https://model.inferx.net/v1`; tenant paths are not hard-coded. No Anthropic Messages, Gemini, or Responses format is documented, so no additional endpoints are stored.

Probes on 2026-10-24 without a key: `GET /v1/models`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`, and `POST /v1/responses` return HTTP 401 `service failure: no tenant context: set a default tenant or use a tenant-restricted API key`. `GET /v1/nonexistent` and `POST /v1/messages` return HTTP 404, as does `POST /anthropic/v1/messages`. A bogus `Authorization: Bearer` returns HTTP 401 `invalid auth token` on both routed and unknown `/v1` paths, so a presented token is checked before routing and does not by itself prove a route exists. On the shared endpoints surface, `GET /endpoints/v1/models` and `POST /endpoints/v1/chat/completions` return the same unauthenticated HTTP 401, while `POST /endpoints/v1/responses`, `POST /endpoints/v1/messages`, and `GET /endpoints/v1/nonexistent` return HTTP 404. Public docs describe only Chat Completions, so the routed-but-undocumented `POST /v1/responses` is not stored.

`GET /v1/models` and `GET /endpoints/v1/models` answer 401 rather than 404, but the public docs do not describe the list shape. No reviewed discovery spec is registered. Model IDs are copied from the console or the published catalog and are not hard-coded. On this date the catalog included `deepseek-v4.1-flash`, `deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017`, `glm-5.3-flash`, and `kimi-k3`. The homepage example uses `Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B`. The [pricing](https://inferx.net/pricing) and [endpoint pricing](https://inferx.net/pricing/endpoints) pages describe a free tier and pay-as-you-go token rates; the catalog does not encode offers.

Only `model.inferx.net` is mapped for host inference. `inferx.net` and `www.inferx.net` serve the marketing site, including `/v1/...` as HTML, and resolve to `custom`, as do lookalike hosts. Keys are copied from the Client Setup section of an endpoint or model page after signing in. The [console guide](https://inferx.net/docs/console-guide) does not publish a stable direct key-list URL, so the template portal is the console sign-in at <https://model.inferx.net/login> with kind `console`.

## Oxion AI — 2026-10-24

`oxion-ai` / `Oxion AI` is a gateway Provider for [Oxion AI](https://oxionai.com), an OpenAI-compatible aggregator. The [Getting Started](https://oxionai.com/docs/getting-started) guide gives the Base URL `https://oxionai.com/v1` and calls `POST https://oxionai.com/v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer` keys that look like `cc_` followed by 48 characters. Keys are created under Dashboard → API Keys, served at [`/dashboard/api-keys`](https://oxionai.com/dashboard/api-keys) (unsigned requests redirect to [`/login`](https://oxionai.com/login)). The documented example model is `gemma-2-2b`. Arca therefore uses Chat Completions as the default protocol on `https://oxionai.com/v1`.

The [coding agents guide](https://oxionai.com/docs/coding-agents) documents an Anthropic-compatible surface for Claude Code and says the same key is accepted as `Authorization: Bearer` or `x-api-key`. Its sample sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=https://oxionai.com/v1`. Probes on 2026-10-24: `POST /v1/messages` with no key returns HTTP 401 in the Anthropic error envelope (`Missing API key.`); a bogus `x-api-key` or `Authorization: Bearer` returns HTTP 401 `Invalid or revoked API key`. `POST /v1/v1/messages` returns HTTP 404, so the live Messages path is `/v1/messages` on the bare host. Arca stores Anthropic Messages at `https://oxionai.com`, which is the base an Anthropic client uses when it appends `/v1/messages`. The sample's `/v1` suffix would send that client to `/v1/v1/messages`, which this probe shows is not routed. No Gemini format is documented. `POST /v1/responses` returns HTTP 404 with or without a bogus key, so no Responses endpoint is stored. `POST /v1/embeddings` is documented and out of scope for chat models.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential. The [Models reference](https://oxionai.com/docs/models) documents `GET https://oxionai.com/v1/models` with `Authorization: Bearer`, returning `{object: "list", data: [{id, object: "model", owned_by, context_window, ...}]}`. [Authentication](https://oxionai.com/docs/authentication) requires the `models:read` scope, which new keys include by default. Probes without a key: `GET /v1/models`, `GET /v1/models/gemma-2-2b`, and `POST /v1/chat/completions` return HTTP 401 `missing_api_key`; a bogus Bearer key returns HTTP 401 `invalid_api_key`. No models are hard-coded. The public [catalog](https://oxionai.com/models) listed 33 models on this date, including the documented example `gemma-2-2b`; the live list is the authority.

Only `oxionai.com` is mapped for host inference. `www.oxionai.com` also answers `GET /v1/models` with HTTP 401, but the docs use the apex host, so `www` and lookalike hosts resolve to `custom`. The [pricing page](https://oxionai.com/pricing) describes a free tier of 0.5M tokens per month with no credit card and paid plans from $3.50; the catalog does not encode offers.

## SCNet — 2026-10-24

`scnet` / `SCNet (超算互联网)` is a gateway Provider for the national supercomputing internet's model API (超算互联网). The [quickstart](https://www.scnet.cn/help/docs/mainsite/mass/tutorial/quickstart.html) and the equivalent [call guide](https://www.scnet.cn/ac/openapi/doc/2.0/moduleapi/tutorial/apicall.html) give the OpenAI-compatible Base URL `https://api.scnet.cn/api/llm/v1` and call `POST https://api.scnet.cn/api/llm/v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer`. The same pages give the Anthropic-compatible Base URL `https://api.scnet.cn/api/llm/anthropic` and call `POST https://api.scnet.cn/api/llm/anthropic/v1/messages` with `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`. Arca therefore defaults to Chat Completions at `https://api.scnet.cn/api/llm/v1` and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://api.scnet.cn/api/llm/anthropic`. Those two paths share the origin `https://api.scnet.cn`, so the saved connection uses that origin with `/api/llm/v1/chat/completions` and `/api/llm/anthropic/v1/messages`. No Gemini format is documented. The docs do not describe an OpenAI Responses API, so none is stored.

Pay-as-you-go keys usually start with `sk-` and TokenPlan keys with `sk-tp-`. The [TokenPlan FAQ](https://www.scnet.cn/ac/openapi/doc/2.0/moduleapi/plans/faq.html) uses the same two Base URLs and says the key types cannot be mixed. Like other shared PAYG/plan endpoints, one template covers both; host inference prefers this template and does not invent a second plan id.

The [model list](https://www.scnet.cn/help/docs/mainsite/mass/api/modulequery.html) documents `GET https://api.scnet.cn/api/llm/v1/models` with `Authorization: Bearer`, returning an OpenAI-shaped `{object: "list", data: [{id, object: "model", owned_by}]}`. Discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential. No models are hard-coded. The documented example id is `DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B` (`owned_by: deepseek`); the Anthropic example uses `MiniMax-M2.5`.

Probes on 2026-10-24 without a key: `POST /api/llm/v1/chat/completions`, `GET /api/llm/v1/models`, and `POST /api/llm/v1/responses` return HTTP 400 `Authorization格式错误，正确格式为Bearer <APIKey>`. `POST /api/llm/anthropic/v1/messages` with no auth header returns HTTP 400 `x-api-key不能为空`. A bogus `Authorization: Bearer` or `x-api-key` on the chat, models, responses, and messages paths returns HTTP 401 `Authentication Failed`. An unknown `/api/llm/v1/nonexistent` returns a different gateway HTTP 401 `用户未登录`, so `/v1/responses` is routed and authenticated even though it is undocumented and not encoded. Arca's Anthropic curl uses `x-api-key`, which this probe shows the messages path accepts at the auth layer. The official curl samples and the Node SDK note also show `Authorization: Bearer`.

Only `api.scnet.cn` is mapped for host inference. `www.scnet.cn` (mall and console), `scnet.cn`, and lookalike hosts resolve to `custom`. Keys are created in the signed-in console after choosing a team, service type, and model scope ([API key guide](https://www.scnet.cn/help/docs/mainsite/mass/tutorial/apikeymgt.html)). That guide does not publish a stable deep link to the key page, so the template portal is the console at <https://www.scnet.cn/ui/console> with kind `console`, not a guessed key route. The mall at <https://www.scnet.cn/ui/mall/> is the marketplace and is not the portal.

## Yeta — 2026-10-24

`yeta` / `Yeta` is a gateway Provider for [Yeta Platform](https://platform.yeta.ai), an OpenAI-compatible API for leading AI models. The platform's signed-in **Docs** page (`/docs`) gives the Base URL `https://platform.yeta.ai/v1` and documents `POST /v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer` API keys, created on the **API keys** page at `/keys`. The example uses model `apex-flash-1`. Arca therefore uses Chat Completions as the default protocol on `https://platform.yeta.ai/v1`. The docs also document an asynchronous video-generation endpoint (`POST /v1/videos`), which is out of scope for Arca chat models; no Responses, Anthropic Messages, or Gemini format is documented, so no additional endpoints are stored.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential: `GET /v1/models` returns an OpenAI-shaped `{data: [...]}` list on the same Base URL, but an unauthenticated request returns HTTP 401 `Invalid token`, so the catalog needs a key. No models are hard-coded. Only `platform.yeta.ai` is mapped for host inference; `yeta.ai` and `www.yeta.ai` resolve to `custom`. The landing page mentions a trial promotion (top up \$1 and get \$10 of credit), but the catalog does not encode offers.

## AnyRouter — 2026-10-24

`anyrouter` / `AnyRouter` is a gateway Provider for [AnyRouter](https://anyrouter.dev), an OpenAI-compatible model router. The [Quickstart](https://docs.anyrouter.dev/getting-started/quickstart) gives the Base URL `https://anyrouter.dev/api/v1` for `POST /api/v1/chat/completions`, with `provider/model` ids such as `minimax/minimax-m3.1-flash` (optionally suffixed `:free`, per the [MiniMax M3.1 Flash post](https://anyrouter.dev/blog/minimax-m3-1-flash-free)). [Authentication](https://docs.anyrouter.dev/getting-started/authentication) sends `Authorization: Bearer` keys prefixed `sk-ar-`, created on the [API Keys dashboard](https://anyrouter.dev/dashboard/keys). Arca uses Chat Completions as the default protocol.

The [Responses guide](https://docs.anyrouter.dev/guides/migrate-to-responses) documents `POST /api/v1/responses` on the same Base URL, and the [Anthropic guide](https://docs.anyrouter.dev/guides/migrate-from-anthropic) documents `POST /api/v1/messages` with SDK base URL `https://anyrouter.dev/api` (Anthropic SDKs append `/v1/messages`). Both are stored as additional endpoints.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential: `GET /api/v1/models` returns an OpenAI-shaped `{data: [...]}` list without a key. No models are hard-coded. Only `anyrouter.dev` is mapped for host inference; `www.anyrouter.dev` resolves to `custom`.

## NoMask — 2026-10-23

`nomask` / `NoMask` is a gateway Provider for [NoMask](https://nomask.ai), an OpenAI-compatible inference API. The [Quickstart](https://nomask.ai/api/doc/quickstart) gives the Base URL `https://nomask.ai/api/v1` and sends `Authorization: Bearer` API keys prefixed `nmk_live_`, created after signing up at [`/register`](https://nomask.ai/register). The [Models](https://nomask.ai/api/doc/models) page documents `POST /v1/chat/completions`, so Arca uses Chat Completions as the default protocol on `https://nomask.ai/api/v1`. No Responses, Anthropic Messages, or Gemini format is documented, so no additional endpoints are stored.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential: `GET /v1/models` is expected to return an OpenAI-shaped `{data: [...]}` list on the same Base URL. No models are hard-coded. Only `nomask.ai` is mapped for host inference; `www.nomask.ai` resolves to `custom`.

## RunInfra — 2026-10-23

`runinfra` / `RunInfra` is a gateway Provider for [RunInfra](https://runinfra.ai), an OpenAI-compatible inference API. The provider base URL is `https://api.runinfra.ai/v1` for `POST /v1/chat/completions`, sent with `Authorization: Bearer` API keys created at [`/settings/api-keys`](https://runinfra.ai/settings/api-keys). Arca therefore uses Chat Completions as the default protocol on that Base URL. No Responses, Anthropic Messages, or Gemini format is documented, so no additional endpoints are stored.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential: `GET /v1/models` is expected to return an OpenAI-shaped `{data: [...]}` list on the same Base URL. No models are hard-coded. Only `api.runinfra.ai` is mapped for host inference; `runinfra.ai` and `www.runinfra.ai` resolve to `custom`.

## GonkaRouter — 2026-10-22

`gonkarouter` / `GonkaRouter` is a gateway Provider for [GonkaRouter](https://gonkarouter.io), an AI model router on the Gonka network. The [Developer Documentation](https://gonkarouter.io/docs) gives the Base URL `https://api.gonkarouter.io` and accepts either `x-api-key` or `Authorization: Bearer` with keys created in the [Dashboard](https://gonkarouter.io/dashboard) (sign in with email or Google). It documents `POST /v1/messages` with the full Anthropic Messages surface (streaming and tool use), pointing Anthropic SDKs and Claude Code at the bare host, and `POST /v1/chat/completions` one-to-one with OpenAI, with OpenAI clients using `https://api.gonkarouter.io/v1`. Arca therefore uses `https://api.gonkarouter.io/v1` with Chat Completions as the default and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://api.gonkarouter.io`. Probes without a key: `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/messages`, and `GET /v1/models` return HTTP 401 `missing Authorization or x-api-key header` (a bogus Bearer key gets `invalid API key`), while `/v1/responses` and unknown paths such as `/v1/nonexistent` return 404, so no Responses endpoint is encoded and no Gemini format is documented.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with a required credential: `GET /v1/models` is routed and authenticated (401, unlike the 404 of unknown paths) but is not described in the docs, so it needs a key. The [Pricing](https://gonkarouter.io/pricing) page lists `zai-org/GLM-5.3-Flash`, `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731`, and `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M2.7` at one per-token rate, with a one-time free credit for new accounts; the docs use slashed, case-sensitive ids (for example `MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M2.7`). No models are hard-coded. Only `api.gonkarouter.io` is mapped for host inference; `gonkarouter.io` and `www.gonkarouter.io` resolve to `custom`.

## KRAFTON Raon — 2026-10-22

`krafton-raon` / `KRAFTON Raon` is an official Provider for KRAFTON's Raon Console API, which serves KRAFTON's own Raon models. The [Getting started](https://raon-mini.krafton.ai/en/docs) page gives the base URL `https://raon-api.krafton.ai`, sends the key as `Authorization: Bearer <key>` (the docs export it as `RAON_API_KEY`; the Anthropic `x-api-key` header is also accepted), and makes its first request with `POST https://raon-api.krafton.ai/v1/chat/completions` and `"model": "raon-1-mini-preview"`. Its OpenAI SDK examples set `base_url="https://raon-api.krafton.ai/v1"`, so Arca defaults to Chat Completions at `https://raon-api.krafton.ai/v1`. The same page lists `POST /v1/responses` (OpenAI Responses, used by Codex) and `POST /v1/messages` (Anthropic Messages, used by Claude Code), and states that requests are not translated between formats. The [Messages](https://raon-mini.krafton.ai/en/docs/messages) page and the troubleshooting section configure the Anthropic SDK and Claude Code with `https://raon-api.krafton.ai` without `/v1`, so Responses is stored at `https://raon-api.krafton.ai/v1` and Anthropic Messages at `https://raon-api.krafton.ai`. No Gemini format is documented. Keys are created on the console's [API keys](https://raon-console.krafton.ai/api-keys) page; per the [FAQ](https://raon-mini.krafton.ai/en), console access is granted by applying at `https://raon-mini.krafton.ai/en#apply`, which emails an invitation.

The [OpenAPI contract](https://raon-mini.krafton.ai/openapi.yaml) describes `GET /v1/models` as an OpenAI-compatible list (`{object: "list", data: [{id, object: "model", ...}]}`) scoped to the caller's organization and extended with display name, context window and pricing. Probes without a key return 401 `invalid_api_key` on every path, including `/v1/models`, `GET /` and unknown paths (Messages returns the Anthropic error shape), so discovery uses the OpenAI adapter with a required credential. The docs and the [model card](https://huggingface.co/KRAFTON/Raon-1-mini-preview) name `raon-1-mini-preview` (open weights); the docs advise reading the ID from `/v1/models` because preview models can change, so no model is hard-coded. The FAQ and the Price section say the API is free for a limited time during the preview, with per-user token and request limits; the catalog does not encode offers. Only `raon-api.krafton.ai` is mapped for host inference; `krafton.ai`, `raon-console.krafton.ai` and `raon-mini.krafton.ai` resolve to `custom`.

## 0G Private Computer — 2026-10-20

`0g-private-computer` / `0G Private Computer` is a gateway Provider for [0G Private Computer](https://pc.0g.ai), 0G Labs' web front end for the 0G Compute Router, which routes each request to TEE-attested providers on the decentralized 0G Compute Network and bills against an on-chain or USD balance. The [Router overview](https://docs.0g.ai/developer-hub/building-on-0g/compute-network/router/overview) gives the mainnet API endpoint `https://router-api.0g.ai/v1` (testnet uses a separate UI, endpoint, balance and keys at `https://router-api-testnet.integratenetwork.work/v1`), and the [Quickstart](https://docs.0g.ai/developer-hub/building-on-0g/compute-network/router/quickstart) calls `POST https://router-api.0g.ai/v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer sk-YOUR_API_KEY` and configures the OpenAI SDK with that Base URL, so Arca defaults to Chat Completions at `https://router-api.0g.ai/v1`. The [Authentication](https://docs.0g.ai/developer-hub/building-on-0g/compute-network/router/authentication) page says inference uses `sk-` API keys created at pc.0g.ai → Dashboard → API Keys (served at [`/dashboard/api-keys`](https://pc.0g.ai/dashboard/api-keys)); `mk-` management keys cannot call inference. The [Router API reference](https://0gfoundation.github.io/0g-router/) documents `POST /v1/chat/completions`, `POST /v1/responses` and `POST /v1/messages`, and the [Claude Code guide](https://docs.0g.ai/developer-hub/building-on-0g/compute-network/router/clients/claude-code) sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=https://router-api.0g.ai`, so Responses is stored at `https://router-api.0g.ai/v1` and Anthropic Messages at `https://router-api.0g.ai`. Format support is per model: each `GET /v1/models` entry carries `supported_formats` (`openai`, `responses`, `anthropic`), and many models list only `openai`. No Gemini format is documented.

Probes without a key: `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `POST /v1/messages` return 401 `missing API key` (`gateway_error`), `POST /v1/responses` returns 401 `missing_authorization`, while `POST /anthropic/v1/messages`, `GET /` and `/v1/nonexistent` return 404. `GET /v1/models` is public, as the [Models](https://docs.0g.ai/developer-hub/building-on-0g/compute-network/router/models) page states, and returns an OpenAI-shaped `{data: [{id, ...}]}` list (129 entries on this date), so discovery uses the OpenAI adapter with an optional credential. On this date the list included `minimax-m3.1-flash` (MiniMax-M3.1-Flash, `supported_formats: ["openai"]`, 1M context, 524,288 max completion tokens, `TeeTLS`, listed at $0.15 input / $0.60 output per 1M tokens). A [0G Labs post](https://x.com/0G_labs/status/2112651128911589725) announces that model free for 3 days from 2026-10-21 13:00 UTC with no balance needed, only an API key; the catalog does not encode offers, and no model is hard-coded. Only `router-api.0g.ai` is mapped for host inference; `0g.ai`, `pc.0g.ai` and the testnet host resolve to `custom`.

## Maximo AI — 2026-10-20

`maximo-ai` / `Maximo AI` is an official Provider for [Maximo AI](https://maximoai.co)'s first-party model API (Atlas, Pandora and Decisions families). The [Atlas 1.5 announcement](https://maximoai.co/news/introducing-maximo-atlas-1-5) (dated 2026-10-18) calls `POST https://api.maximoai.co/v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer $MAXIMO_API_KEY`, and its OpenAI SDK examples set `base_url="https://api.maximoai.co/v1"`. The [API reference](https://maximoai.co/platform/docs) gives the same Base URL and also documents a stateful, OpenAI-compatible Responses API (`POST /v1/responses`, `GET`/`DELETE /v1/responses/{response_id}`) on the same models and billing. Arca therefore defaults to Chat Completions at `https://api.maximoai.co/v1`, which the announcement and quick starts lead with, and stores Responses on the same Base URL. No Anthropic Messages or Gemini format is documented, so neither is stored. The reference's Authentication section shows an `x-api-key` header, but its own request samples and the OpenAI SDK guidance use `Authorization: Bearer`, which is what Arca's OpenAI protocols send. Keys are managed at [`/platform/keys`](https://maximoai.co/platform/keys); `https://maximoai.co/docs` also links into the docs, but the API reference itself is served at `/platform/docs`.

Probes without a key: `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `POST /v1/responses` return HTTP 401 (`Unauthorized: Valid API Key or Token required.`, a bogus Bearer or `x-api-key` gets the same), while `POST /v1/messages`, `POST /anthropic/v1/messages`, `GET /` and `/v1/nonexistent` return 404. `GET /v1/models` is public and returns the OpenAI-shaped `{data: [{id, ...}]}` catalog, and the reference tells callers to read each model's supported parameters there, so discovery uses the OpenAI adapter with an optional credential. On this date it listed `maximo-atlas-1.5` (1M context, 128K max output; $0.20 input / $0.03 cached input / $1.00 output per 1M tokens, matching the announcement), `maximo-atlas-1.4`, `maximo-atlas-1.4-ultra-fast`, `maximo-atlas-1.3` (sunset 2026-11-02, replaced by `maximo-atlas-1.5`), `maximo-pandora-3.9`, `maximo-pandora-3.9-nano`, `maximo-pandora-3.8-pro` and the non-chat `maximo-decisions-1`. No model is hard-coded. The announcement offers $5 in free API credits to new accounts; the catalog does not encode offers. Only `api.maximoai.co` is mapped for host inference; `maximoai.co` and `www.maximoai.co` resolve to `custom`.

## IndicStack — 2026-10-20

`indicstack` is a gateway Provider for [IndicStack](https://indicstack.ai), operated by IndicStack Consultancy Services LLP (Bengaluru), which serves hosted open-weight models on Indian infrastructure with per-token INR billing and GST invoices. The [homepage](https://indicstack.ai) and [Platform](https://indicstack.ai/platform) page configure the OpenAI SDK with `base_url="https://api.indicstack.ai/v1"`, `api_key="isk_your_api_key"` and `client.chat.completions.create(model="qwen38-27b", ...)`, and the [OpenAI migration guide](https://indicstack.ai/blog/migrate-openai-to-india-hosted-api) calls `POST https://api.indicstack.ai/v1/chat/completions` with `Authorization: Bearer isk_your_api_key`, so Arca uses that Base URL with Chat Completions as the default. Keys are issued from the [console](https://console.indicstack.ai/signup); `indicstack.ai/docs` redirects there, so there are no public API docs beyond these pages. The [Models](https://indicstack.ai/models) page lists `qwen38-27b` (Qwen3.8 27B, dense, FP8, text/image/video input, 262K context, Apache 2.0) as the only live model. No Responses, Anthropic Messages or Gemini format is documented, so no additional endpoints are stored. Probes without a key: `POST /v1/chat/completions` returns 401 `authentication_error` `Invalid API key` (a bogus `isk_` Bearer key gets the same), `GET /` and `/anthropic/v1/messages` return 404 `Not found. Use /v1/ endpoints.`, and an unknown `/v1/nonexistent` returns 404 `Model or endpoint not found`, while `/v1/responses`, `/v1/messages` and `GET /v1/models` return 401.

No reviewed discovery spec is registered: `GET /v1/models` answers 401 rather than 404, but its availability and response shape are undocumented, so model IDs fall back to the generic OpenAI catalog attempt or manual entry, and nothing is hard-coded. Only `api.indicstack.ai` is mapped for host inference; `indicstack.ai`, `www.indicstack.ai`, and `console.indicstack.ai` resolve to `custom`.

## Constellation Gate — 2026-10-19

`constellation-gate` is a gateway Provider for [Constellation Gate](https://constellationgate.ai) (Gate, built by Constellation Network), a model gateway that puts prompt-injection screening, redaction, spend limits, caching, and an audit trail in front of hundreds of models. The [Authentication](https://docs.constellationgate.ai/getting-started/authentication/) page gives the Base URL `https://gateway.constellationgate.ai`, says OpenAI-compatible clients use the `/v1` suffix while Anthropic-style clients keep the bare host and target `/v1/messages`, and issues `sk-gw-` keys from the [dashboard](https://app.constellationgate.ai) under API keys, sent as `Authorization: Bearer sk-gw-...`. The [API reference](https://docs.constellationgate.ai/api-reference/) lists exactly two model surfaces on the gateway: Anthropic Messages at `/v1/messages` and OpenAI Chat Completions at `/v1/chat/completions`. The [OpenAI SDK guide](https://docs.constellationgate.ai/guides/openai-sdk/) uses `baseURL: "https://gateway.constellationgate.ai/v1"` with `GATE_API_KEY` and `provider/model` ids, and the [Claude Code guide](https://docs.constellationgate.ai/guides/claude-code/) sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to `https://gateway.constellationgate.ai` with the Gate key as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Arca therefore uses `https://gateway.constellationgate.ai/v1` with Chat Completions as the default and stores Anthropic Messages at `https://gateway.constellationgate.ai`. Probes without a key: `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `POST /v1/messages` return HTTP 401 `invalid_key` (`Missing gateway API key ... via X-Gate-Api-Key, Authorization: Bearer <sk-gw-...>, or x-api-key: sk-gw-...`), and a bogus `sk-gw-` key in either `Authorization: Bearer` or `x-api-key` gets `Invalid or revoked API key.`. Unknown paths such as `/v1/nonexistent` return the same 401 before routing, so the 401 on `/v1/responses` does not show a Responses endpoint; it is undocumented and not encoded, and no Gemini format is documented. The bring-your-own-key passthrough (`X-Gate-Api-Key` plus `X-Gate-Upstream-Url`) needs custom headers and is not encoded.

Model discovery uses the OpenAI catalog decoder with an optional credential: the API reference documents `GET /v1/models` as listing the models available to the organization, and an anonymous probe returned HTTP 200 with an OpenAI-shaped `{object: "list", data: [...]}` of 401 entries (`id`, `owned_by`, `name`, `context_window`, `max_tokens`, `pricing`, optional `models_dev_id`).
